\tilde{F}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\equiv\tilde{F}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and
\tilde{H}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\equiv\tilde{H}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
are defined by (
2.16
) in terms of
F_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and
H_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
,

(2)

F_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
is an effective force, in particular
F_{\kappa,0}=F_{\kappa}
,

(3)

H_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
is defined by (
2.8
) in terms of
F_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
.

Let
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
be the map defined by (
2.27
) in terms of
\tilde{F}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\equiv\tilde{F}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and
\tilde{H}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\equiv\tilde{H}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and suppose that
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})
is a fixed point of
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
. Then one shows that the limit

\lim_{\mu\searrow 0}K_{\mu}\ast\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu}=:\varPhi_{\kappa}

exists in
C(\mathbb{T})
and satisfies the equation
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
. Moreover, if
\tilde{F}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and
\tilde{H}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
converge in appropriate sense as
\kappa\searrow 0
, then
\varPhi_{\kappa}
converges in the Besov space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{M})
. For details see Appendix
B
.

Remark 2.17
.

The bounds (
2.25
) and (
2.26
) stated in the above lemma say that the functionals
\tilde{F}_{\mu}
and
\tilde{H}_{\mu}
are compatible with the growth of the norm
\|\tilde{\varPhi}_{\mu}\|
when
\mu
tends to zero. The bounds also take into account the fact that there is one power of
\lambda^{1/3}
for each factor of
\varphi
in the expression (
2.1
) for the force
F_{\kappa}[\varphi]
.

Sketch of the proof.

First note that for
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\in\mathscr{B}_{R}
it holds

\begin{gathered}{}^{\sigma-\alpha}\,\|\tilde{F}_{\mu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\mu}]\|\leq R,\qquad[\mu]^{\sigma-\beta}\,\|\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\mu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\mu}]\cdot(\tilde{G}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{\zeta}_{\mu})\|\leq C_{G},\qquad[\mu]^{\sigma-\beta}\,\|\tilde{H}_{\mu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\mu}]\|\leq 1.\end{gathered}

(2.28)

By Remark
2.13
we obtain

\|\mathbf{Q}(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})\|_{\mathscr{B}_{R}}\leq C_{G}\,\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-\alpha}\int_{\mu}^{1}\|\tilde{F}_{\eta}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\eta}]+\tilde{\zeta}_{\eta}\|\,\mathrm{d}\eta\\
+R\,\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}\,[\mu]^{-\beta}\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\tilde{H}_{\eta}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\eta}]+\mathrm{D}\tilde{F}_{\eta}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\eta}]\cdot(\tilde{G}_{\eta}\ast\tilde{\zeta}_{\eta})\|\,\mathrm{d}\eta\\
\leq\sigma/|\alpha|~C_{G}\,R+\sigma/|\alpha|~C_{G}\,R+\sigma/\beta~R+\sigma/\beta~C_{G}\,R\leq R^{2}.

(2.29)

By similar estimates one shows that
\mathbf{Q}\,:\,\mathscr{B}_{R}\to\mathscr{B}_{R}
is a contraction with the Lipschitz constant less than
1/2
.
∎

Remark 2.18
.

Note that the map
\mathbf{Q}
is only a contraction provided
\lambda
is sufficiently small. In the case of parabolic equation an analogous map
\mathbf{Q}
is a contraction for all values of
\lambda
provided the time interval in which we solve the equation is sufficiently small.

3
Construction of effective force

In the previous section, we argued that, under certain assumptions, the equation

\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}],\qquad F_{\kappa}[\varphi]:=\xi_{\kappa}+\lambda\,\varphi^{3}+\sum_{i=1}^{i_{\sharp}}\lambda^{i}\,c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\varphi,

(3.1)

which we want to solve, can be formulated as a fixed point problem for the map
\mathbf{Q}
defined by (
2.27
). Recall that the map
\mathbf{Q}
involves a scale decomposition
(G_{\mu})_{\mu\in[0,1]}
of the Green function
G
and two families of functionals
(\tilde{F}_{\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}
and
(\tilde{H}_{\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}
. The scale decomposition of the Green function was fixed in Def.
2.9
. By Remark
2.16
a fixed point of the map
\mathbf{Q}
corresponds to a solution of the original equation if

(\tilde{F}_{\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}\equiv(\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]},\qquad\qquad(\tilde{H}_{\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}\equiv(\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}

are defined in terms of an effective force

(F_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in[0,1]}

by the following equations

\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]:=K_{\mu}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}[K_{\mu}\ast\varphi],\qquad\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]:=K_{\mu}\ast H_{\kappa,\mu}[K_{\mu}\ast\varphi]

(3.2)

and

H_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]:=\partial_{\mu}F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]+\mathrm{D}F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]\cdot(\dot{G}_{\mu}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]).

(3.3)

In view of Lemma
2.15
, for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
we would like to construct an effective force

(F_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in[0,1]}

such that for some random
R\in[1,\infty]
with finite moments of all orders it holds

[\mu]^{\sigma-\alpha}\,\|\mathrm{D}^{k}\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]\cdot\psi^{\otimes k}\,\|\leq R\,(|\lambda|^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\psi\|)^{k}\,(1/2+|\lambda|^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\varphi\|)^{m_{\flat}},

(3.4)

[\mu]^{\sigma-\beta}\,\|\mathrm{D}^{k}\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]\cdot\psi^{\otimes k}\,\|\leq\lambda^{1/3}\,R^{2}\,(|\lambda|^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\psi\|)^{k}\,(1/2+|\lambda|^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\varphi\|)^{m_{\flat}}

(3.5)

for all
k\in\{0,1,2\}
,
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
,
\varphi,\psi\in C(\mathbb{T})
and
\lambda\in[-1,1]
.

Remark 3.1
.

After establishing the above-mentioned result we will be able to conclude that for every
\kappa\in(0,1]
there exists
\varPhi_{\kappa}
such that
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
and almost surely
\|\varPhi_{\kappa}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{M})}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
. In order to prove almost sure convergence of
\varPhi_{\kappa}
as
\kappa\searrow 0
in the Besov space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{M})
one has to show in addition that the functionals
\tilde{F}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and
\tilde{H}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
converge as
\kappa\searrow 0
in the sense specified in Exercise
B.2
.

As we will see,
F_{\kappa,\mu}
is in some sense a small perturbation of the noise
\xi_{\kappa}
and we expect to have the bound (
3.4
) for all
\alpha<\sigma-d/2\leq 0
provided the mass counterterm is chosen appropriately. The main problem is to ensure that the bound (
3.5
) holds for some
\beta>0
. The idea is to define an effective force
F_{\kappa,\mu}
so that
H_{\kappa,\mu}=O(\lambda^{i_{\flat}+1})
for a sufficiently big
i_{\flat}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. The hope is that for such
F_{\kappa,\mu}
the bound (
3.5
) may be true for some
\beta>0
.

The starting point of the construction of an effective force is the ansatz

\langle F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\flat}}\sum_{m=0}^{3i}\lambda^{i}\,\langle F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle

(3.6)

for all
\psi,\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})
. The distributions
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
that appear on the RHS of the above equality are called the effective force coefficients. By definition
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
are such that the expression
\langle F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle
is invariant under permutations of the test functions
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\ \mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})
. The coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
of the force
F_{\kappa}
are defined by an equality analogous to (
3.6
). Note that by (
3.3
) we have

\langle H_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{2i_{\flat}}\sum_{m=0}^{3i}\lambda^{i}\,\langle H^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle

(3.7)

for some
H^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
. Recall that we want to construct the effective force such that
H_{\kappa,\mu}=O(\lambda^{i_{\flat}+1})
, which implies
H^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}+1\}
.

Remark 3.2
.

Let us list the non-vanishing force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
:

\begin{gathered}F^{0,0}_{\kappa}(x)=\xi_{\kappa}(x),\qquad F^{1,3}_{\kappa}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\mathrm{d}y_{2},\mathrm{d}y_{3})=\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{2})\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{3}),\\
F^{i,1}_{\kappa}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1})=c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1}),\quad i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\},\end{gathered}

(3.8)

where
(c_{\kappa}^{(i)})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
are the so-called counterterms. It is easy to see that
F^{0,0}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{V}^{0}
,
F^{1,3}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{V}^{3}
,
F^{i,1}_{\kappa}\in\mathcal{V}^{1}
, where the spaces
\mathcal{V}^{m}
are introduced in the definition below.

Definition 3.3
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the space
\mathcal{V}^{m}
consists of maps
V\,:\,\mathbb{M}\times\mathrm{Borel}(\mathbb{M}^{m})\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

for every
A\in\mathrm{Borel}(\mathbb{M}^{m})
the map
x\mapsto V(x;A+x)
is continuous and
2\pi
periodic, where
A+x:=\{(y_{1}+x,\ldots,y_{m}+x)\in\mathbb{M}^{m}\,|\,(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in A\}
for
A\subset\mathbb{M}^{m}
,

(2)

for every
x\in\mathbb{M}
the map
A\mapsto V(x;A)
is a measure with finite total variation,

(3)

the following norm

\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{M}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{m}}|V(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m})|

(3.9)

is finite.

Remark 3.4
.

(\mathcal{V}^{m},\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}})
is a Banach space. For every
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
and
A\in\mathrm{Borel}(\mathbb{M}^{m})
the map
x\mapsto V(x;A)
is measurable. We identify
V\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
with a distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
, denoted by the same symbol, defined by the measure
V(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{d}y_{m})\,\mathrm{d}x
.

Since we demand that
H_{\kappa,\mu}=O(\lambda^{i_{\flat}+1})
, by (
3.3
) it holds

\partial_{\mu}F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]+\mathrm{D}F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]\cdot(\dot{G}_{\mu}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi])=H_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]=O(\lambda^{i_{\flat}+1}).

(3.10)

As a result, the effective force coefficients
(F^{i,m}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfy the following flow equation

\partial_{\mu}F_{\kappa,\mu}^{i,m}=-\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\,(1+k)\,\mathbf{B}(\dot{G}_{\mu},F^{j,1+k}_{\kappa,\mu},F^{i-j,m-k}_{\kappa,\mu}),

(3.11)

where the map
\mathbf{B}
is introduced below.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
. The map
\mathbf{B}\,:\,\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})\times\mathcal{V}^{1+k}\times\mathcal{V}^{m-k}\to\mathcal{V}^{m}
is defined by

\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{d}y_{m})\\
:=\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{2}}W(x;\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}y_{\pi(1)},\ldots,\mathrm{d}y_{\pi(k)})\,G(y-z)\,U(z;\mathrm{d}y_{\pi(k+1)},\ldots,\mathrm{d}y_{\pi(m)})\,\mathrm{d}z.

(3.12)

Exercise 3.1
.

Prove that the map
\mathbf{B}:\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})\times\mathcal{V}^{1+k}\times\mathcal{V}^{m-k}\to\mathcal{V}^{m}
is well defined and

\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|G\|_{\mathcal{K}}\,\|W\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1+k}}\|U\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m-k}}.

(3.13)

The basic idea behind the flow equation approach is a recursive construction of the effective force coefficients
(F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu})_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in[0,1]
. For each
\kappa\in(0,1]
we define the coefficients in such a way that the map

[0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m}

(3.14)

is continuous and continuously differentiable for
\mu\in(0,1]
using the following recursive algorithm:

(0)

We set
F^{0,0}_{\kappa,\mu}=\xi_{\kappa}
and
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
if
m>3i
,

(I)

Assuming that all
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
were constructed we define
\dot{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
to be the RHS of (
3.11
).

(II)

Subsequently,
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
is defined by

F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\dot{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

Definition 3.6
.

The finite list of the effective force coefficients
(F^{i,m}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}}
is called the
enhanced noise
.

Remark 3.7
.

The above procedure cannot be used to construct directly
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
\kappa=0
. In fact, we expect that
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\notin\mathcal{V}^{m}
. For example,
F^{0,0}_{0,\mu}=\xi\notin C(\mathbb{T})=\mathcal{V}^{0}
. Instead, the coefficients
F^{i,m}_{0,\mu}
are defined probabilistically. The stochastic estimates for the enhanced noise are stated in Theorem
3.14
below.

Remark 3.8

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Note that the effective force coefficients depend implicitly on the counterterms
(c^{(i)}_{\kappa})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
. More specifically, the coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\{2,3,\ldots\}
depend on
(c^{(j)}_{\kappa})_{j\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\wedge(i-1)\}}
and the coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\{0,1\}
depend on
(c^{(j)}_{\kappa})_{j\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\wedge i\}}
.

Exercise 3.2
.

Check that the condition
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
if
m>3i
is consistent with the conditions stated in Items (I) and (II). Prove that
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
for all
m>(2i+1)\wedge 3i
.

Remark 3.9
.

Let us list some examples of effective force coefficients. To this end, it will be convenient to use a diagrammatical notation. We view the diagrams as placeholders for certain multi-linear functionals of the noise. Since the diagrams do not play any role in the flow equation approach we refrain from defining precise rules that are used to draw them. Instead, for each diagram we provided an explicit expression it represents. Note that the edges of the diagrams that are introduced below represent the fluctuation propagator
(G-G_{\mu})(x-y)=:\,x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure74.pdf}}}}\,y
and not the Green function
G(x-y)=:\,x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure75.pdf}}}}\,y
. First note that

\begin{gathered}F^{0,0}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)=\xi_{\kappa}(x)=:\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.90712pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure76.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(x),\\
F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\mathrm{d}y_{2},\mathrm{d}y_{3})=F^{1,3}_{\kappa}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\mathrm{d}y_{2},\mathrm{d}y_{3})=:\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.90712pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure77.pdf}}}}\,(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\mathrm{d}y_{2},\mathrm{d}y_{3}).\end{gathered}

(3.15)

Let

\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure78.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x):=((G-G_{\mu})\ast\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.90712pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure79.pdf}}}}_{\kappa})(x)\,,\qquad\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure80.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x):=(\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure81.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x))^{2}+c_{\kappa}^{(1)}/3.

(3.16)

We have

\begin{gathered}F^{1,2}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\mathrm{d}y_{2})=3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure82.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)\,\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{2})=:3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 2.96065pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure83.pdf}}}}_{\,\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\mathrm{d}y_{2}),\\
F^{1,1}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1})=3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure84.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)\,\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1})=:3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 2.96065pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure85.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1}),\\
F^{1,0}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)=(\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure86.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x))^{3}+c_{\kappa}^{(1)}\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure87.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)=:\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure88.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x),\end{gathered}

(3.17)

The coefficient

F^{2,5}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{d}y_{5})=:3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 2.96065pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure89.pdf}}}}_{\,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{d}y_{5})

(3.18)

coincides with the symmetric part of

3\,\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{2})\,(G-G_{\mu})(x-y_{3})\,\delta_{y_{3}}(\mathrm{d}y_{4})\delta_{y_{3}}(\mathrm{d}y_{5})\,\mathrm{d}y_{3}.

(3.19)

The construction of coefficients
F^{2,4}_{\kappa,\mu}
,
F^{2,3}_{\kappa,\mu}
,
F^{2,2}_{\kappa,\mu}
is left as an exercise. Let

\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure90.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa,\mu}(x):=((G-G_{\mu})\ast\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure91.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu})(x).

(3.20)

The remaining second order coefficients are given by

F^{2,1}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1})=9\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure92.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)\,(G-G_{\mu})(x-y_{1})\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure93.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(y_{1})\,\mathrm{d}y_{1}+c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\,\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\\
+6\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure94.pdf}}}}_{\,\kappa,\mu}(x)\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure95.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa,\mu}(x)\,\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y_{1})=:9\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure96.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1})+6\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 4.66782pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure97.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y_{1})

(3.21)

and

F^{2,0}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)=3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure98.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure99.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa,\mu}(x)+c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure100.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x)=:3\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure101.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}(x).

(3.22)

Exercise 3.3
.

Convince yourself that the expression given in Remark
3.9
satisfy the condition
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
, where
F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
are the force coefficients listed in Remark
3.2
.

Exercise 3.4
.

Using the notation
\dot{G}_{\mu}(x-y):=\,x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure102.pdf}}}}\,y
draw the diagrams representing the coefficients
\partial_{\mu}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
. Verify the formulas given in Remark
3.9
and write explicit expressions for the coefficients
F^{2,4}_{\kappa,\mu}
,
F^{2,3}_{\kappa,\mu}
,
F^{2,2}_{\kappa,\mu}
. For simplicity, you can ignore numerical prefactors.

Definition 3.10
.

Let
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
and
\alpha\equiv\alpha_{\varepsilon}:=\sigma-d/2-\varepsilon
,
\gamma\equiv\gamma_{\varepsilon}:=3\sigma-d-3\varepsilon
. For
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m):=\alpha_{\varepsilon}-\sigma-m\,\alpha_{\varepsilon}+i\,\gamma_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}.

(3.23)

We omit
\varepsilon
if
\varepsilon=0
. Let
i_{\flat},i_{\sharp}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be the smallest positive integers such that
\varrho(i_{\flat}+1,0)>0
,
\varrho(i_{\sharp}+1,1)>0
, respectively.

Remark 3.11
.

Note that
\alpha_{\varepsilon}\leq 0
for all
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
. Moreover,
\gamma_{\varepsilon}>0
for all
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\varepsilon=0
by the condition of subcriticality. In particular,
i_{\flat},i_{\sharp}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
are well defined and there are only finitely many
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
m\leq 3i
and
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
. Recall that if
m>3i
, then
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
vanishes identically. For arbitrary
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
m\leq 3i
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)<\varrho(i,m)
.

Remark 3.12

(
\spadesuit
)
.

We claim that there exists
\varepsilon_{\diamond}\in(0,\sigma)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
and all
i,m,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+l>0
if
\varrho(i,m)+l>0
. In what follows, we assume that
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
.

Definition 3.13
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
let
\mathcal{K}^{n}\subset\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{n})
be the space of signed measures on
\mathbb{M}^{n}
with finite total variation. We set
\|K\|_{\mathcal{K}^{n}}=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{n}}|K(\mathrm{d}x_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n})|
. Given
K\in\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}^{1}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we set
K^{\otimes n}:=K\otimes\ldots\otimes K\in\mathcal{K}^{n}
.

In the theorem below we state the
stochastic estimates
for the enhanced noise. Recall that the enhanced noise coincides with the following finite list of the effective force coefficients
(F^{i,m}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}}.
By the deterministic results established in Section
2
and Corollary
3.17
these estimates imply that for every
\kappa\in(0,1]
there exists
\varPhi_{\kappa}
such that
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
and
\mathbb{E}\big(\sup_{\kappa\in(0,1]}\|\varPhi_{\kappa}\|^{n}_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{M})}\big)<\infty
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For the proof of the convergence of
\varPhi_{\kappa}
as
\kappa\searrow 0
see Appendix
B
.

Theorem 3.14
.

There exist a choice of the counterterms
(c^{(i)}_{\kappa})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
in the expression (
1.7
) for the force
F_{\kappa}
and a random variable
\tilde{R}\in[1,\infty]
such that
\mathbb{E}\tilde{R}^{n}<\infty
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and it holds

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\tilde{R}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}

(3.24)

for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
,
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

The theorem follows from the bounds for the cumulants of the effective force coefficients established in Theorem
5.3
and Exercise
5.6
together with a Kolmogorov-type argument from Lemma
A.1
.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

Actually, if the bound stated in the above is known for the relevant effective force coefficients, i.e.
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
such that
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
, it can be easily proved deterministically for the irrelevant coefficients, i.e.
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
such that
\varrho(i,m)>0
. This is the subject of Exercise
3.5
below. Since the coefficients
F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i\in\{i_{\sharp}+1,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
are irrelevant no renormalization should be necessary to bound them. This is an intuitive reason why only the counterterms
(c_{\kappa}^{(i)})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
are included in the expression for the force (
1.7
).

Remark 3.16
.

The exponent
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
that appears in the bound in the above theorem follows from the application of the naive rules of power counting. Let us analyse an example. For concreteness, suppose that
d=5
and
\sigma=2
. Then
\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 4.66782pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure103.pdf}}}}_{\,\kappa=0}=G\ast\xi\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{M})
with
\alpha=\sigma-d/2-\varepsilon=-1/2-\varepsilon
. The above theorem implies in particular that

\big\|K_{\mu}\ast\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure104.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}\big\|\lesssim[\mu]^{-1/2-2\varepsilon}

(3.25)

almost surely uniformly in
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
. Note that the tree
\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure105.pdf}}}}_{\kappa,\mu}
depends on
\mu\in(0,1]
. Consequently, the above bound says nothing about the regularity of the above tree. Note that it would completely hopeless to prove an analogous bound for the tree

\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.53802pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure106.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(x):=\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure107.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(x)\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure108.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa}(x)+1/3~c_{\kappa}^{(2)}~\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure109.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(x)

(3.26)

with the standard edges
\,x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure110.pdf}}}}\,y\,=G(x-y)
, which do not depend on
\mu\in(0,1]
. The regularity of the above tree cannot possibly be better than the regularity of
\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.2452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure111.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}\in\mathscr{C}^{-1-\varepsilon}
. In fact, we have the following almost sure bound

\big\|K_{\mu}\ast\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure112.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}\big\|\lesssim[\mu]^{-1-\varepsilon}

(3.27)

uniform in
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
, which is insufficient to close the estimates. For this reason, in the approach to singular SPDEs involving paracontrolled distributions one bounds instead the tree

\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure113.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}:=\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure114.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}\odot\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure115.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa}+1/3~c_{\kappa}^{(2)}~\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure116.pdf}}}}_{\kappa},

(3.28)

where
\odot
denotes the so-called resonant product. The above tree satisfies the bound

\big\|K_{\mu}\ast\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure117.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}\big\|\lesssim[\mu]^{-1/2-\varepsilon}

(3.29)

uniform in
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
. On the other hand, in the regularity structure framework one studies the recentered tree

\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure118.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(x,y):=\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 4.3729pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure119.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(y)\,\Big(\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure120.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa}(y)-\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure121.pdf}}}}_{\!\!\kappa}(x)\Big)+1/3~c_{\kappa}^{(2)}~\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure122.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(y)

(3.30)

and proves that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{M}}\left|\int K_{\mu}(x-y)\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 4.3729pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure123.pdf}}}}_{\kappa}(x,y)\,\mathrm{d}y\right|\lesssim[\mu]^{-1/2-\varepsilon}

(3.31)

uniformly in
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
. The trees that represent the effective force coefficients does not involve any recentering. Consequently, the structure group does not enter the flow equation framework and there is no positive renormalization. The intuitive reason why the bound (
3.25
) holds true is the fact that the edges
\,x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 4.95404pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure124.pdf}}}}\,y\,=(G-G_{\mu})(x-y)
depend on the scale parameter
\mu\in(0,1]
and are in some sense small for small
\mu
, for example,

\|G-G_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\dot{G}_{\eta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{\sigma}.

Corollary 3.17
.

There exists deterministic
c\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\lambda\in[-1,1]
the functionals
\tilde{F}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
and
\tilde{H}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
defined in terms of the enhanced noise by (
3.6
), (
3.3
) and (
3.2
) satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
2.15
with
R=c\,\tilde{R}
,
\alpha=\sigma-d/2-\varepsilon
,
\beta=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\flat}+1,0)
,
m_{\flat}=3i_{\flat}
for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
, where
\tilde{R}\in[1,\infty]
is the random variable introduced in Theorem
3.14
.

Proof
\mathrm{(}\spadesuit\mathrm{)}
.

We have to verify the bounds (
3.4
), (
3.5
). Recall that

\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]=K_{\mu}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}[K_{\mu}\ast\varphi],\qquad\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]=K_{\mu}\ast H_{\kappa,\mu}[K_{\mu}\ast\varphi]

and define

\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}:=K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\tilde{H}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}:=K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast H^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}.

Then
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
and
\tilde{H}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
are related to
\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu}
and
\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu}
by formulas analogous to (
3.6
) and (
3.7
). By Theorem
3.14
we have

\|\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\tilde{R}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}.

(3.32)

Verification of the bound (
3.4
) for
\tilde{F}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
is straightforward. Let us prove the bound (
3.5
) for
\tilde{H}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
. Using (
3.3
), (
3.7
) as well as the fact that the effective force coefficients satisfy the flow equation (
3.11
) we obtain

H^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=\sum_{j=i-i_{\flat}}^{i_{\flat}}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\,(1+k)\,\mathbf{B}(\dot{G}_{\mu},F^{j,1+k}_{\kappa,\mu},F^{i-j,m-k}_{\kappa,\mu})

(3.33)

for
i\in\{i_{\flat}+1,\ldots,2i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Since
\mathbf{P}_{\mu}K_{\mu}=\delta_{0}
this implies that

\tilde{H}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=\sum_{j=i-i_{\flat}}^{i_{\flat}}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\,(1+k)\,\mathbf{B}(\tilde{G}_{\mu},\tilde{F}^{j,1+k}_{\kappa,\mu},\tilde{F}^{i-j,m-k}_{\kappa,\mu}),

(3.34)

where
\tilde{G}_{\mu}=\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2}\dot{G}_{\mu}
. Consequently, by the bounds for
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
, the estimate stated in Exercise
3.1
and the identity

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(j,1+k)+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i-j,m-k)=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma

(3.35)

we have

\|\tilde{H}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim\tilde{R}^{2}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma}.

(3.36)

Since

\langle\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle:=\sum_{i=i_{\flat}+1}^{2i_{\flat}}\sum_{m=0}^{3i}\lambda^{i}\,\langle\tilde{H}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle

(3.37)

we obtain the bound (
3.5
) with
\beta=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{\flat}+1,0)>0
.
∎

Exercise 3.5
.

Check that the bound stated in Theorem
3.14
cannot be proved deterministically for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by following the steps (0), (I), (II) of the recursive algorithm presented above. More specifically, assume that the bound holds true for all
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i<i_{\circ}
, or
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. Prove that

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\dot{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma}

for
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
, where
\dot{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
denotes the RHS of (
3.11
). By Exercise
2.1
(3)

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\|K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\dot{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(3.38)

Conclude that
\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}
provided
\varrho(i,m)>0
. The upshot is that the so-called irrelevant coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
such that
\varrho(i,m)>0
can be bounded deterministically. For the relevant coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
such that
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
the above procedure fails.

Lemma 3.18
.

For all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that for all
s\in\{0,1\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in[0,1/2]
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\subset\{(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}\,|\,|x-y_{1}|\vee\ldots\vee|x-y_{m}|\leq c\,[\mu]\}.

(3.39)

Remark 3.19
.

To prove the theorem is it enough to use the graphical representation for
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
introduced in Remark
3.9
and observe that for
\mu\in(0,1/2]
the fluctuation propagator
(G-G_{\mu})(x-y)
represented by edges of the graphs vanishes identically if
|x-y|>[\mu]
by Remark
2.10
. Since we did not introduce precise rules for drawing diagrams in the exercise below we suggest to prove this result using the flow equation. Note that the above support property is not true for
\mu
close to one.

Exercise 3.6

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Prove the above lemma by induction using the flow equation (
3.11
). Hint: Observe that
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
is local, and consequently it satisfies the above support property. Note that for
\mu\in(0,1/2]
the kernel
\dot{G}_{\mu}
is supported in a ball of radius
[\mu]
.

Remark 3.20

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Since we would like to use Lemma
3.18
in the proof of the stochastic estimates for the enhanced noise we will actually establish the bound stated in Theorem
3.14
only for
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. The bound for
\mu\in[1/2,1]
can be then easily proved deterministically. Alternatively, one can set

\langle F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi],\psi\rangle:=\sum_{i=0}^{i_{\flat}}\sum_{m=0}^{3i}\lambda^{i}\,\langle F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu\wedge 1/2},\psi\otimes\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle.

(3.40)

Then
H_{\kappa,\mu}=O(\lambda^{i_{\flat}+1})
is true for
\mu\in(0,1/2]
, which is sufficient to show the bound (
3.5
) for some
\beta>0
.

4
Cumulants of effective force coefficients

Recall that the finite list of the effective force coefficients
(F^{i,m}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}}
is called the enhanced noise. In order to prove the stochastic estimates for the enhanced noise stated in Theorem
3.14
we will first establish certain bounds for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients. Subsequently, we will infer bounds for the moments and apply a Kolmogorov-type argument to conclude. The bounds for cumulants involve the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}
introduced in Def.
4.5
and are stated in Theorem
5.3
. The proof of the bounds for cumulants is based on a certain flow equation that is derived in Lemma
4.14
.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I=\{1,\ldots,p\}
and
\zeta_{q}
,
q\in I
, be random variables. The joint cumulant of the multi-set
(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p})
is defined by

\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p})\equiv\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}=(-\mathrm{i})^{p}\partial_{t_{1}}\ldots\partial_{t_{p}}\log\mathbb{E}\exp(\mathrm{i}t_{1}\zeta_{1}+\ldots+\mathrm{i}t_{p}\zeta_{p})\big|_{t_{1}=\ldots=t_{p}=0}.

(4.1)

In particular,
\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2})-\mathbb{E}\zeta_{1}\,\mathbb{E}\zeta_{2}
.

Definition 4.2
.

Given
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I=\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and random distributions
\zeta_{q}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m_{q}})
,
q\in I
, the deterministic distribution

\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I}\equiv\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n})\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{n}\times\mathbb{M}^{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}})

is defined by the equality

\langle\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n}),\psi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\psi_{n}\otimes\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{n}\rangle:=\mathbb{E}(\langle\zeta_{1},\psi_{1}\otimes\varphi_{1}\rangle,\ldots,\langle\zeta_{n},\psi_{n}\otimes\varphi_{n}\rangle)

(4.2)

for all
\psi_{q}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})
,
\varphi_{q}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M}^{m_{q}})
,
q\in I
.

Definition 4.3
.

A list
(i,m,s,r)
, where
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
s,r\in\{0,1\}
is called an index. For
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we call

\mathtt{I}=((i_{1},m_{1},s_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},s_{n},r_{n}))

(4.3)

a list of indices. We define
n(\mathtt{I}):=n
,
i(\mathtt{I}):=i_{1}+\ldots+i_{n}
,
\mathfrak{m}(\mathtt{I}):=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
,
m(\mathtt{I}):=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
,
s(\mathtt{I}):=s_{1}+\ldots+s_{n}
and
r(\mathtt{I}):=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{n}
. We use the following notation for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients

E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}:=\mathbb{E}(\partial_{\mu}^{s_{1}}\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}F^{i_{1},m_{1}}_{\kappa,\mu},\ldots,\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{n}}\partial_{\mu}^{s_{n}}F^{i_{n},m_{n}}_{\kappa,\mu})\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{n(\mathtt{I})+m(\mathtt{I})}).

(4.4)

Remark 4.4
.

In order to prove the convergence of the enhanced noise as
\kappa\searrow 0
one has to study cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
r(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
. In what follows, for simplicity, we restrict attention to cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
\mathtt{I}=((i_{1},m_{1},s_{1},0),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},s_{n},0))
.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathfrak{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
and
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
. The vector space
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
consists of maps
V\in C(\mathbb{M}^{n}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
such that

(1)

the function

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\mapsto V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{y}_{1}+x_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{n}+x_{n})

is
2\pi
periodic in all variables for every

(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=(\mathrm{y}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{n})\in\mathbb{M}^{m_{1}}\times\ldots\times\mathbb{M}^{m_{n}}=\mathbb{M}^{m},

where

\mathrm{y}+x:=(y_{1}+x,\ldots,y_{n}+x)\in\mathbb{M}^{m}

for arbitrary
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
x\in\mathbb{M}
,
\mathrm{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{m}
,

(2)

it holds

V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})=V(x_{1}+z,\ldots,x_{n}+z;y_{1}+z,\ldots,y_{m}+z)

for all
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots,y_{m},z\in\mathbb{M}
,

(3)

the norm

\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}:=\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{M}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}}|V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\,\mathrm{d}x_{2}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n}\,\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m}

(4.5)

is finite.

Remark 4.6
.

Note that for
n=1
,
\mathfrak{m}=m\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
and
V\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathrm{t}}
it holds
\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathrm{t}}}=\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}
.

Remark 4.7
.

Using the condition of translational invariance stated in Item (2) of the above definition the expression for the norm
\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}
can be rewritten in more symmetric form

\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}}|V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\,\mathrm{d}x_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}x_{n}\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m}.

(4.6)

Definition 4.8
.

For
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
and a list of indices
\mathtt{I}
of the form (
4.3
) we define

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I}):=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{1},m_{1})+\ldots+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i_{n},m_{n})\in\mathbb{R}.

(4.7)

We also set
\varrho(\mathtt{I}):=\varrho_{0}(\mathtt{I})
.

Remark 4.9
.

Using the fact that the law of the noise
\xi_{\kappa}
is invariant under translations in space one proves that the same is true for the effective force coefficients
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
. Since
\tilde{K}_{\mu}\in C(\mathbb{M})\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{M})
and
\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m}
for all
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
one easily shows that

\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}

for all
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
, where
n=n(\mathtt{I})
,
m=m(\mathtt{I})
and
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{m}(\mathtt{I})
. In Theorem
5.3
stated in the next section we prove that for an appropriate choice of the counterterms the following bound

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})+d(n-1)}

(4.8)

holds uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. In order to see that, at least at a heuristic level, the above estimate is compatible with the bound stated in Theorem
3.14
suppose that

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\lesssim[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s)}.

The last bound is almost what Theorem
3.14
says. Observe that this bounds implies that

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})},

(4.9)

where

\|V\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}:=\sup_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathbb{M}}\int|V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\,\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m}.

(4.10)

Actually, the bound (
4.9
) would be also true if
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
was defined to be the expected value of a product of the effective force coefficients and not their joint cumulant. The presence of the extra factor
[\mu]^{d(n-1)}
appearing in the bound (
4.8
) can be understood at least for
\mu\geq\kappa
by noting the following support property of the cumulants

\mathrm{supp}\,E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\subset\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{n+m}\,|\,\\
|x_{2}-x_{1}|\vee\ldots\vee|x_{n}-x_{1}|\vee|y_{1}-x_{1}|\vee\ldots\vee|y_{m}-x_{1}|\leq c\,[\kappa\vee\mu]\}

(4.11)

for all
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
and some
c\in(0,\infty)
depending only on
\mathtt{I}
. Note that the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathrm{t}}}
is weaker than
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}
. Because of the extra factor
[\mu]^{d(n-1)}
the bound (
4.8
) is easier to establish than the bound (
4.9
).

Remark 4.10

(
\spadesuit
)
.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
and any list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
m(\mathtt{I})\leq 3i(\mathtt{I})
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})<\varrho(\mathtt{I})
. Moreover,
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)d>0
for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\diamond})
and lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)d>0
.

Exercise 4.1
.

Draw the graphs representing cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
\mathrm{i}(\mathtt{I})=2
,
m(\mathtt{I})=2
,
s(\mathtt{I})\in\{0,1\}
,
r(\mathtt{I})=0
. For simplicity, ignore numerical prefactors. Use the graphical representation of the effective force coefficients in terms of trees introduced in Section
3
. Recall that
x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 1.33803pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure125.pdf}}}}\,y\,=(G-G_{\mu})(x-y)
and
x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure126.pdf}}}}\,y\,=\dot{G}_{\mu}(x-y)
. Furthermore, use the edge
x\,\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\hbox{\lower 3.0452pt\hbox{{\lxSVG@includegraphics{}{}{figures-ext/spde_flow_notes-figure127.pdf}}}}\,y\,:=\mathbb{E}\xi_{\kappa}(x)\xi_{\kappa}(y)
to denote the covariance of the regularized noise. Note that, in general, graphs for cumulants are not trees. Hint: For the warm-up, draw the diagrams for the cumulant
\mathbb{E}(\xi_{\kappa}(x_{1})\xi_{\kappa}(x_{2})\xi(x_{3}),\xi_{\kappa}(y_{1})\xi_{\kappa}(y_{2}),\xi_{\kappa}(z))
.

Definition 4.11
.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\hat{n}\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n+1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{m}=(m_{1}+m_{n+1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n},\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{m}}=(1+m_{1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{n+1})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n+1},\\
\hat{\mathsf{m}}=(1+m_{1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{\hat{n}})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\hat{n}},\qquad\check{\mathsf{m}}=(m_{\hat{n}+1},\ldots,m_{n+1})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n-\hat{n}+1}\end{gathered}

(4.12)

and
\underline{m}=m_{1}+m_{n+1}
. The bilinear map
\mathbf{A}\,:\,\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\tilde{\mathsf{m}}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathsf{m}}
is defined by

\mathbf{A}(G,V)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{y}_{1},\mathrm{y}_{n+1},\mathrm{y}_{2},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{n})\\
:=\int_{\mathbb{M}^{2}}V(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n+1};y,\mathrm{y}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{n+1})\,G(y-x_{n+1})\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x_{n+1}.

(4.13)

The trilinear map
\mathbf{B}\,:\,\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\hat{\mathsf{m}}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\check{\mathsf{m}}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathsf{m}}
is defined by

\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\mathrm{y}_{1},\mathrm{y}_{n+1},\mathrm{y}_{2},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{n})\\
:=\frac{1}{\underline{m}!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{\underline{m}}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{2}}W(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\hat{n}};y,y_{\pi(1)},\ldots,y_{\pi(m_{1})},\mathrm{y}_{2},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{\hat{n}})\,G(y-x_{n+1})\\
\times\,U(x_{n+1},x_{\hat{n}+1},\ldots,x_{n};y_{\pi(m_{1}+1)},\ldots,y_{\pi(\underline{m})},\mathrm{y}_{\hat{n}+1},\ldots,\mathrm{y}_{n})\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x_{n+1}.

(4.14)

In the above equations
\mathrm{y}_{j}\in\mathbb{M}^{m_{j}}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,n+1\}
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I=\{1,\ldots,p\}
and
\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{p},\Phi,\Psi
be random variables. It holds

\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1}\ldots\zeta_{p})=\sum_{r=1}^{p}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{1},\ldots,I_{r}\subset I,\\
I_{1}\cup\ldots\cup I_{r}=I\\
I_{1},\ldots,I_{r}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{1}}\ldots\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{r}},

(4.15)

\mathbb{E}((\zeta_{q})_{q\in I},\Phi\Psi)=\mathbb{E}((\zeta_{q})_{q\in I},\Phi,\Psi)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I_{1},I_{2}\subset I\\
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=I\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}((\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{1}},\Phi)~\mathbb{E}((\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{2}},\Psi).

(4.16)

Proof.

See e.g. Proposition 3.2.1 in
[
PT11
]
.
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
i_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m_{1},\ldots,m_{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
I\equiv\{2,\ldots,n\}
. For any random distributions
\zeta_{q}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m_{q}})
,
q\in I
, the cumulant

\mathbb{E}(\partial_{\mu}F^{i_{1},m_{1}}_{\kappa,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I})\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{n+m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}})

(4.17)

is a linear combination of the expressions

\mathbf{A}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},\mathbb{E}(F^{j,1+k}_{\kappa,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I},F^{i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k}_{\kappa,\mu})\big)

(4.18)

or

\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},\mathbb{E}(F^{j,1+k}_{\kappa,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{1}}),\mathbb{E}(F^{i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k}_{\kappa,\mu},(\zeta_{q})_{q\in I_{2}})\big),

(4.19)

where
j\in\{1,\ldots,i_{1}\}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m_{1}\}
,
r\in\{0,r_{1}\}
and the subsets
I_{1},I_{2}\subset I
are such that
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=I
and
I_{1}\cap I_{2}=\emptyset
. The coefficients of the above linear combination do not depend on
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
. We used the notation introduced in Def.
4.2
.

Proof.

The statement follows immediately from the flow equation (
3.11
) and (
4.16
).
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\mathtt{J}\equiv(\mathtt{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{n})=((i_{1},m_{1},s_{1},0),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},s_{n},0))
be a list of indices such that
s_{1}=1
.

(A)

The distribution
E^{\mathtt{J}}_{\kappa,\mu}
can be expressed as a linear combination of distributions of the form

\mathbf{A}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},E^{\mathtt{K}}_{\kappa,\mu}\big)\qquad\textrm{or}\qquad\mathbf{B}\big(\dot{G}_{\mu},E^{\mathtt{L}}_{\kappa,\mu},E^{\mathtt{M}}_{\kappa,\mu}\big),

(4.20)

where the lists of indices
\mathtt{K}
,
\mathtt{L}
,
\mathtt{M}
satisfy the following conditions

\begin{split}&n(\mathtt{K})=n(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&i(\mathtt{K})=i(\mathtt{J}),\\
&m(\mathtt{K})=m(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&s(\mathtt{K})=s(\mathtt{J})-1,\\
&\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{J})-\sigma=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{K})-d,\end{split}\qquad~\textrm{or}\qquad\quad\begin{split}&n(\mathtt{L})+n(\mathtt{M})=n(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&i(\mathtt{L})+i(\mathtt{M})=i(\mathtt{J}),\\
&m(\mathtt{L})+m(\mathtt{M})=m(\mathtt{J})+1,\\
&s(\mathtt{L})+s(\mathtt{M})=s(\mathtt{J})-1,\\
&\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{J})-\sigma=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{L})+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{M}).\end{split}

(4.21)

(B)

Suppose that the bound

\|\tilde{K}^{\otimes(n(\mathtt{I})+m(\mathtt{I}))}_{\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}(\mathtt{I})}}\\
\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)d}

(4.22)

holds uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1/2]
for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})<i(\mathtt{J})
, or
i(\mathtt{I})=i(\mathtt{J})
and
m(\mathtt{I})>m(\mathtt{J})
. Then the above bound holds uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1/2]
for
\mathtt{I}=\mathtt{J}
.

Remark 4.15
.

Let
\mathtt{J}\equiv(\mathtt{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{n})
be a list of indices. For a permutation
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{n}
we set
\pi(\mathtt{J}):=(\mathtt{J}_{\pi(1)},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{\pi(n)})
. By Remark
4.7
it holds

\|E^{\mathtt{J}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}(\mathtt{J})}}=\|E^{\pi(\mathtt{J})}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}(\pi(\mathtt{J}))}}.

Hence, the above lemma is true for all list of indices
\mathtt{J}
such that
s(\mathtt{I})\neq 0
.

Proof
\mathrm{(}\spadesuit\mathrm{)}
.

Part (A) of the lemma follows immediately from Lemma
4.13
applied with

\zeta_{q}\equiv\partial_{\mu}^{s_{q}}F^{i_{q},m_{q},a_{q}}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad q\in\{2,\ldots,n\}.

(4.23)

It holds

\begin{gathered}\mathtt{K}=((j,k+1,0,0),\,\mathtt{J}_{2},\ldots,\mathtt{J}_{n},\,(i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k,0,0)),\\
\mathtt{L}=(j,k+1,0,0)\sqcup(\mathtt{J}_{q})_{q\in I_{1}},\qquad\mathtt{M}=(i_{1}-j,m_{1}-k,0,0)\sqcup(\mathtt{J}_{q})_{q\in I_{2}},\end{gathered}

(4.24)

where
\sqcup
denotes the concatenation of lists,
I_{1}\cup I_{2}=I=\{2,\ldots,n\}
,
I_{1}\cap I_{2}=\emptyset
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,i_{1}\}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,m_{1}\}
coincide with the respective objects in (
4.18
) and (
4.19
). This together with Def. (
4.8
) implies that the lists
\mathtt{K}
,
\mathtt{L}
,
\mathtt{M}
satisfy the conditions stated in Part (A). To prove Part (B) we use Part (A) and Remark
4.18
.
∎

In the remaining part of this section, we collect some technical results that were used in the proof of the above lemma.

Definition 4.16

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
\mathbb{T}:=\mathbb{M}/(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{d}
. For
K\in L^{1}(\mathbb{M})
we define
\mathbf{T}K\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T})
by

\mathbf{T}K(x):=\sum_{y\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{d}}K(x+y).

(4.25)

Lemma 4.17

(
\spadesuit
)
.

The maps
\mathbf{A}:\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
,

\mathbf{B}:\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
are well defined. It holds

\|\mathbf{A}(G,V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\leq\|\mathbf{T}|G|\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}}},

(4.26)

\|\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\leq\|G\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}\,\|W\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\,\|U\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}}}.

(4.27)

Proof.

See
[
Duc25b
, Lemma 14.10]
.
∎

Remark 4.18

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Note that Lemmas
2.11
and
4.19
(D) imply that
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}^{2}\dot{G}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}\lesssim 1
and

\|\mathbf{T}|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}^{2}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}|\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\,\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}^{3}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{M})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d}\,\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}^{3}_{\mu}\dot{G}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T})},

(4.28)

uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
. Moreover, using the fact that
\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}\tilde{K}_{\mu}=\delta_{0}
one shows that for all
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast\mathbf{A}(G,V)=\mathbf{A}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{P}}^{2}_{\mu}G,\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m+2)}\ast V\big)

(4.29)

and

\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast\mathbf{B}(G,W,U)=\mathbf{B}\big(\tilde{\mathbf{P}}^{2}_{\mu}G,\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(\hat{n}+\hat{m}+1)}\ast W,\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n-\hat{n}+m-\hat{m}+1)}\ast U\big),

(4.30)

where
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n+1}
and
\hat{m}=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{\hat{n}}
. Consequently, by the above lemma we have

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast\mathbf{A}(\dot{G}_{\mu},V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}}}

(4.31)

and

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast\mathbf{B}(\dot{G}_{\mu},W,U)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(\hat{n}+\hat{m}+1)}\ast W\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n-\hat{n}+m-\hat{m}+1)}\ast U\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}}}

(4.32)

uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
and
V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
,

W\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
U\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\check{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

Lemma 4.19

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
and
p\in[1,\infty]
. The following is true:

(A)

If
|a|\leq d
, then
\|\partial^{a}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-|a|}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(B)

It holds
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-\sigma}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(C)

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{M})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d(p-1)/p}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(D)

\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d(p-1)/p}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Exercise 4.2

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Prove the above lemma. Hint for Item (D): Identify
\mathbb{T}
with
[-\pi,\pi)^{d}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Write
\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}=\tilde{K}_{\mu}+(\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}-\tilde{K}_{\mu})
. Use the fact that
\tilde{K}_{1}(x)\lesssim\exp(-|x|)
uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
to show that

\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}-\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim[\mu]^{q}

for any
p\in[1,\infty]
and
q>0
.

5
Uniform bounds for cumulants

In this section, we establish uniform bounds for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients. The proof is by induction and is based on the flow equation for the cumulants derived in Lemma
4.14
. The cumulants that coincide with the expected values of the coefficients
(F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
will require a special treatment. More specifically, to prove bounds for
\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}
we will decompose
\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y)
into a local part proportional to
\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y)
and a certain remainder using the maps introduced in the following definition.

Definition 5.1
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we define
\delta^{[m]}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
by the equality

\langle\delta^{[m]},\psi\otimes\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle:=\int_{\mathbb{M}}\psi(x)\varphi_{1}(x)\ldots\varphi_{m}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

(5.1)

for all
\psi,\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})
. Let
\mathcal{X}^{a}(x;y):=(x-y)^{a}
for
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
and let
V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}
be such that
V(x;y)=V(x-y;0)
,
V(x;y)=V(-x;-y)
and
\mathcal{X}^{a}V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}
for all
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
. We define
\mathbf{I}V:=\int_{\mathbb{M}}V(x;y)\mathrm{d}y\in\mathbb{R}
. For
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
we define
\mathbf{R}^{a}V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}
by the equality

(\mathbf{R}^{a}V)(x;y):=\frac{|a|}{a!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{|a|-1}/\tau^{d}~(\mathcal{X}^{a}V)(x;x+(y-x)/\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(5.2)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
.

Exercise 5.1
.

Prove that the following equality
V=(\mathbf{I}V)\,\delta^{[1]}+\sum_{|a|=2}\partial^{a}\mathbf{R}^{a}V
holds in
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{2})
, where the sum is over
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
and
\partial^{a}
denotes the derivative with respect to the second argument. Show that
|\mathbf{I}V|\leq\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}
and
\|\mathbf{R}^{a}V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\leq\|\mathcal{X}^{a}V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}
. Hint: Use the integral form of the Taylor remainder.

Remark 5.2
.

The proof of the following theorem uses the idea from the a very simple proof of perturbative renormalizability of QFT models given by Polchinski
[
Pol84
]
based on the renormalization group flow equation (see
[
Mul03
]
for a review). For non-perturbative applications of the flow equation see
[
BK87
,
BB21
]
.

Theorem 5.3
.

There exists a choice of the counterterms
(c^{(i)}_{\kappa})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
in (
1.7
) such that for all list of indices

\mathtt{I}=((i_{1},m_{1},s_{1},0),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},s_{n},0))
the bound

\|\tilde{K}^{\otimes(n+m)}_{\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})+d(n-1)}

(5.3)

holds uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1/2]
, where
\mathfrak{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
,
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
.

Remark 5.4
.

Note that the equation we want to solve,
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
, is invariant under the transformations
(\varPhi_{\kappa},\xi_{\kappa})\mapsto-(\varPhi_{\kappa},\xi_{\kappa})
and
(\varPhi_{\kappa},\xi_{\kappa})\mapsto(\varPhi_{\kappa}(-\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}),\xi_{\kappa}(-\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}))
. Using the fact that the law of
\xi_{\kappa}
is invariant under the transformations
\xi_{\kappa}\mapsto-\xi_{\kappa}
and
\xi_{\kappa}\mapsto\xi_{\kappa}(-\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})
one shows that
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
unless
n(\mathtt{I})+m(\mathtt{I})\in 2\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
is invariant under the inversion through the origin
0\in\mathbb{M}^{n(\mathtt{I})+m(\mathtt{I})}
.

Proof.

We first note that the theorem is trivially true for all list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
m(\mathtt{I})>3i(\mathtt{I})
since then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
. The rest of the proof is by induction.

The base case:

Consider a list of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=0
. In this case the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
coincide with the cumulants of the white noise
\xi_{\kappa}
. The only non-vanishing cumulant is the covariance corresponding to
n(\mathtt{I})=2
,
\mathfrak{m}(\mathtt{I})=(0,0)
,
m(\mathtt{I})=0
and
s(\mathtt{I})=0
. It holds

\|\mathbb{E}(\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\xi_{\kappa},\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\xi_{\kappa})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\leq\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\mathbb{E}(\xi(x_{1})\xi(\mathrm{d}x_{2}))|=1.

(5.4)

This finishes the proof of the base case.

Induction step:

Fix
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Assume that the theorem is true for all lists of indices
\mathtt{I}
such that either
i(\mathtt{I})<i
, or
i(\mathtt{I})=i
and
m(\mathtt{I})>m
. We shall prove the theorem for all
\mathtt{I}
such that
i(\mathtt{I})=i
and
m(\mathtt{I})=m
.

Consider the case
s(\mathtt{I})>0
. Then we use the flow equation for cumulants introduced in the previous section. More precisely, the bound (
5.3
) follows from the inductive assumption and Lemma
4.14
(B).

It remains to prove the statement for lists of indices
\mathtt{I}=((i_{1},m_{1},0),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},0))
such that
s(\mathtt{I})=0
. It follows from Def.
4.3
of the cumulants
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}
that
