E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}=E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,0}+\sum_{q=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\mu}E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(5.5)

where

\mathtt{I}_{q}=((i_{1},m_{1},0,0),\ldots,(i_{q},m_{q},1,0),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},0,0)).

(5.6)

Note that
s(\mathtt{I}_{q})=1
, hence the bound (
5.3
) has already been established for
E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa,\eta}
.

First, let us analyse the
irrelevant cumulants
, i.e. those with
\mathtt{I}
such that

\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)d>0.

(5.7)

Let us recall the non-zero force coefficients

F^{0,0}_{\kappa}=\xi_{\kappa},\quad F^{1,3}_{\kappa}=\delta^{[3]},\quad F^{i,1}_{\kappa}=c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\,\delta^{[1]},\quad i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}.

(5.8)

In particular
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
is deterministic if
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. If
n(\mathtt{I})>1
, then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,0}
is a joint cumulant of a list of at least two random distributions. Since
i(\mathtt{I})=i>0
one of these distributions is deterministic and the cumulant vanishes. If
n(\mathtt{I})=1
and
\varrho(\mathtt{I})>0
, then
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,0}
coincides with
F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
for some
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\varrho(i,m)>0
. However,
F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
vanishes for
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\varrho(i,m)>0
. Hence, we conclude that
E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,0}=0
for all irrelevant cumulants. Using this fact and (
5.5
) we arrive at

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\leq\sum_{q=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}_{q}}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta,

(5.9)

which follows from the estimate proved in Exercise
5.4
. Using the induction hypothesis we arrive at

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma+d(n-1)}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+d(n-1)}.

(5.10)

Note that to get the last bound we crucially used the fact that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})+d(n-1)>0
for sufficiently small
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
. This finishes the proof of the induction step for the irrelevant cumulants.

Next, let us analyse the
relevant cumulants
, i.e. those with
\mathtt{I}
such that

\varrho(\mathtt{I})+(n(\mathtt{I})-1)d\leq 0.

(5.11)

Note that for
\mathrm{i}(\mathtt{I})\geq 1
the above inequality implies that
n(\mathtt{I})=1
. Consequently, by Remark
5.4
the non-trivial cases are
\mathtt{I}=(1,3,0)
or
\mathtt{I}=(i,1,0)
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}
. Thus, we have to prove bounds for the following cumulants

\mathbb{E}F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu},\quad i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}.

(5.12)

Recall that
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
. Since
\partial_{\mu}F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
we have
\mathbb{E}F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\mu}=\delta^{[3]}
. Consequently,

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 4}\ast\mathbb{E}F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{3}}=1\leq[\mu]^{-\varepsilon}=[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1,3)}.

(5.13)

Let us now study the cumulants
\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}
. By Exercise
5.1
the bound for the cumulant

E^{\mathtt{I}_{1}}_{\kappa,\mu}=\mathbb{E}\partial_{\mu}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}=:\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu},

which follows from the induction hypothesis, implies that

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)-\sigma},\qquad|\mathbf{I}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})|\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)-\sigma}.

(5.14)

Using Exercise
5.1
we introduce the following decomposition

\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}=\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\,\delta^{[1]}+\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}+\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{2}),

(5.15)

where
\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu},\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{2})
are defined by the equalities

\begin{split}\partial_{\eta}\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\mathbf{I}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}),\\
\partial_{\eta}\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\textstyle\sum_{|a|=2}\partial^{a}\mathbf{R}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}),\\
\partial_{\eta}\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}-\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta},\end{split}\qquad\qquad\begin{split}\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}&:=c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\\
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}&:=0,\\
\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}&:=0\end{split}

(5.16)

The motivation behind the above decomposition is that, as we shall see below,
\partial_{\eta}\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
and
\partial_{\eta}\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
satisfy the following bound

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\partial_{\eta}\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\vee\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\partial_{\eta}\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\eta/\mu]^{2}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\,[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)-\sigma+2}.

(5.17)

It turns out that for
d\in\{1,\ldots,6\}
,
\sigma\in(d/3,d/2]
and sufficiently small
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2>0
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Consequently, the RHS of the above estimate is integrable in
\eta
at
\eta=0
. On the other hand, the bound for
\partial_{\eta}\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\,\delta^{[1]}
, given in (
5.14
), is not integrable in
\eta
at
\eta=0
but this contribution is local and, as we will prove in a moment,
\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
can be bounded by making a suitable choice of the counterterm
c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Let us first study the local term
\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\,\delta^{[1]}
. We start by fixing the counterterm to be

c_{\kappa}^{(i)}:=-\int_{0}^{1/2}\mathbf{I}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(5.18)

Then it holds

\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}=c_{\kappa}^{(i)}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\mathbf{I}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta=-\int_{\mu}^{1/2}\mathbf{I}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(5.19)

Consequently, since
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)<0
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}
we obtain

|\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}|\lesssim\int_{\mu}^{1/2}\,[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)-\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)}.

(5.20)

This implies the desired bound

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast(\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\,\delta^{[1]})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)}.

(5.21)

Next, let us proceed to the estimates for the non-local terms
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
and
\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
. Using the boundary condition
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}=0
and the bound
\|\partial^{a}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-|a|}
proved in Lemma
4.19
(A) we estimate

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\sup_{|a|=2}\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\mathbf{R}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(5.22)

By Exercise
5.1
the RHS is bounded up to a constant by

\sup_{|a|=2}~[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(5.23)

Recall that by Lemma
3.18
there exists
c\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\eta\in(0,1/2]
the coefficient
\partial_{\eta}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta}
as well as its expected value
\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}=\mathbb{E}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta}
are supported in the set

\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{M}^{2}\,|\,|x-y|\leq c\,[\eta]\}.

(5.24)

Pretending that there exist
c\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\eta\in(0,1/2]
the kernel
\tilde{K}_{\eta}
is supported in

\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<c\,[\eta]\}

we obtain that
\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
has the same support property as
\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
. Consequently,

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta})\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\eta]^{2}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}.

(5.25)

Since the kernel
\tilde{K}_{\eta}
does not have the above-mentioned support property the rigorous proof of the above estimate is slightly more complicated and is given in Lemma
5.6
below. Taking into account the induction hypothesis, we obtain the bound

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{2}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(5.26)

Similarly, by Exercise
5.5
we have

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\tilde{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{2}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast\dot{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(5.27)

Finally, we use the fact that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2>0
to show

[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)},

(5.28)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Exercise 5.2
.

Verify (
5.15
) using Remark
5.4
.

Exercise 5.3
.

Check that in the Da Prato–Debussche regime corresponding to
\sigma\in(2d/5,d/2]
the above proof simplifies as the use of the maps
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{R}
is redundant.

Remark 5.5

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Observe that the counterterms
(c^{(i)}_{\kappa})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
are implicitly fixed by the following renormalization conditions

\int_{\mathbb{M}}\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu=1/2}(x;\mathrm{d}y)=0,\qquad\quad i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}.

(5.29)

The procedure of fixing the counterterms works because of the property of the effective force coefficients mentioned in Remark
3.8
.

Exercise 5.4

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathfrak{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
and
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
. Using Exercise
2.1
(3) prove that for all
0\leq\eta\leq\mu\leq 1
and
V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
it holds

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\leq\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes(n+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}.

(5.30)

Exercise 5.5

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Show that it holds

\|(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}-\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2})\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}\lesssim[\eta/\mu]^{2}~\|\tilde{K}_{\eta}^{\otimes 2}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}}

(5.31)

uniformly over
\mu,\eta\in(0,1]
and
V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{1}
. Hint: Let
\hat{K}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{K}
be the solution of
\hat{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}\hat{K}_{\mu}=\delta_{0}
, where
\hat{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}:=(1-[\mu]^{2}\Delta)
. Verify that
\hat{K}_{\mu}-\hat{K}_{\mu}\ast\hat{K}_{\eta}=[\eta/\mu]^{2}~(\hat{K}_{\eta}-\hat{K}_{\eta}\ast\hat{K}_{\mu})
.

Lemma 5.6

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Fix some
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
. There exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that if for some
\mu\in(0,1/2]
and
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,V\subset\{(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\,|\,|x-y_{1}|\vee\ldots\vee|x-y_{m}|\leq c\,[\mu]\}

(5.32)

then

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{|a|}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}.

(5.33)

Remark 5.7
.

Note that the lemma would be obvious if
\tilde{K}_{\mu}\in C(\mathbb{M})
was compactly supported in a ball
\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<c\,[\mu]\}
for some
c\in(0,\infty)
independent of
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. Even though this is not exactly true, one can easily proved the above result by leveraging the fact that the kernel
\tilde{K}_{\mu}
decays exponentially with the rate
1/[\mu]
.

Proof
\mathrm{(}\spadesuit\mathrm{)}
.

A simple rescaling reduces the proof to the case
\mu=1
. Let
v\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M})
be such that
\mathrm{supp}\,v\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<1\}
and
v=1
on
\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|\leq 1/2\}
. For
\tau\in[1,\infty)
let
L_{\tau}(x):=\tilde{K}_{1}(x)\,v(x/\tau)
. It holds

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\leq\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(L_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\|\mathcal{X}^{a}\partial_{\tau}(L_{\tau}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\|L_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\tau^{|a|}\,\|\partial_{\tau}(L_{\tau}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(5.34)

where to get the last estimate we used the fact that
\mathrm{supp}\,L_{\tau}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<\tau\}
. Next, we observe that

L_{\tau}=\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}L_{\tau}\ast\tilde{K}_{1},\qquad\partial_{\tau}L_{\tau}=\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\partial_{\tau}L_{\tau}\ast\tilde{K}_{1}

(5.35)

and
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}L_{\tau}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim 1
and
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\partial_{\tau}L_{\tau}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim\tau^{-N}
uniformly in
\tau\in[1,\infty)
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
because of the exponential decay of the kernel
\tilde{K}_{1}
. Consequently, we have

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\tilde{K}_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\lesssim\|\tilde{K}_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\tau^{|a|-N}\,\|\tilde{K}_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(5.36)

which finishes the proof.
∎

Exercise 5.6

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Prove that with the choice of the counterterms
(c^{(i)}_{\kappa})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
made in the proof of Theorem
5.3
for all list of indices

\mathtt{I}=((i_{1},m_{1},s_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},m_{n},s_{n},r_{n}))
the bound

\|\tilde{K}^{\otimes(n+m)}_{\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r(\mathtt{I})}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})+d(n-1)}

(5.37)

holds uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. Hint: First generalize appropriately Lemma
4.13
. Then follow the proof of Theorem
5.3
. To prove the base case of the induction verify that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}|\mathbb{E}((\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{1}}\xi_{\kappa})(x)\,(\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r_{2}}\xi_{\kappa})(y))|\,\mathrm{d}y\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)(r_{1}+r_{2})}\,[\mu]^{-2\varepsilon}.

(5.38)

Appendix A
Kolmogorov-type argument

The following lemma completes the proof of the stochastic estimates for the enhanced noise. The crucial input is provided by the bounds for the cumulants of the effective force, which were established in Theorem
5.3
and Exercise
5.6
. The idea is to first infer the bounds for the moments of the effective force coefficients from the bounds for the cumulants and subsequently use a Kolmogorov-type argument.

Lemma A.1
.

Fix
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
d/n<\varepsilon
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
. For
s,r\in\{0,1\}
we define the list of indices
\mathtt{I}\equiv\mathtt{I}(s,r)=((i,m,s,r),\ldots,(i,m,s,r))
,
n(\mathtt{I})=n
. Assume that for
s,r\in\{0,1\}
the following bound

\|\tilde{K}^{\otimes(n+nm)}_{\mu}\ast E^{\mathtt{I}}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}(\mathtt{I})}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})-\sigma s(\mathtt{I})+d(n-1)}

(A.1)

holds uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. Then there exists a random variable
R\in[1,\infty]
such that
\mathbb{E}R^{n}<\infty
and the following bound

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq R\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m)}.

(A.2)

holds for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1/2]
.

Proof
\mathrm{(}\spadesuit\mathrm{)}
.

By the assumption, Exercise
A.1
, the relation between the expectation of a product of random variables and their joint cumulants expressed by (
4.15
) as well as the equalities

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\mathtt{I})=n\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
and
s(\mathtt{I})=ns
we have

\mathbb{E}(\tilde{K}^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\otimes\tilde{K}^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}))^{n}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s-dm)}

(A.3)

uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1/2]
and
x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}\in\mathbb{M}
. Using the Fubini theorem and the argument from the proof of Lemma
5.6
one shows that

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\otimes\tilde{K}^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s-dm)+dm}.

(A.4)

Taking into account the fact that
K_{\mu}=\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{K}_{\mu}
we conclude by Lemma
A.2
that

\mathbb{E}\|K^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s-dm)-d},

(A.5)

Employing again the strategy from the proof of Lemma
5.6
we obtain

\mathbb{E}\|K^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s)-d}.

(A.6)

Using Lemma
4.19
(B) we arrive at

\mathbb{E}\|\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}(K^{\otimes(1+m)}_{\mu}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu})\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\kappa]^{n(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s)-d}.

(A.7)

Finally, we apply the result stated in Exercise
A.2
with

\zeta_{\kappa,2\mu}=K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\rho=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-2\varepsilon.

(A.8)

Since
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
for
m>3i
and
\rho\geq\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m)
for
m\leq 3i
this finishes the proof.
∎

Exercise A.1

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathfrak{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
and
m=m_{1}+\ldots+m_{n}
. Show that it holds

\|\tilde{K}^{\otimes(n+m)}_{\mu}\ast V\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}^{n+m})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d(n+m-1)}\,\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}}

(A.9)

uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
and
V\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathfrak{m}}
. Hint: Use Lemma
4.19
(C) and (D) with
p=\infty
.

Lemma A.2

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all random fields
\zeta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
and
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}\leq C\,[\mu]^{-d(1+m)}\,\mathbb{E}\|\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}

(A.10)

Proof.

Note that

\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\zeta=(\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\otimes\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes m})\star\zeta

(A.11)

where
\star
is the convolution in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}
and
\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}
is the periodisation of
\tilde{K}_{\mu}
(see Def.
4.16
). Using the Young inequality for convolutions we obtain

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}(\mathbb{T})}^{n}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}(\mathbb{M})}^{mn}\,\mathbb{E}\|\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{T})}.

(A.12)

The lemma follows now from Lemma
4.19
(C), (D).
∎

Exercise A.2

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Prove that there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that if

\mathbb{E}\|\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}^{\phantom{l}}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\\
\leq C\,[\kappa]^{n(-\sigma r+\varepsilon r)}[\mu]^{n(\rho-\sigma s+\varepsilon s)},\qquad s,r\in\{0,1\},~\kappa,\mu\in(0,1],

(A.13)

for some differentiable random function
\zeta:(0,1]^{2}\to\mathcal{V}^{m}
and
C>0
,
\rho\leq 0
, then

\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-n\rho}\,\|\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\Big)\leq c\,C.

(A.14)

Hint: Use the estimate

[\mu]^{-\rho}\,\|\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|\zeta_{1,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}+\int_{\mu}^{1}[\eta]^{-\rho}\,\|\partial_{\eta}\zeta_{1,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta+\int_{\mu}^{1}\int_{\kappa}^{1}[\eta]^{-\rho}\,\|\partial_{\eta}\partial_{\nu}\zeta_{\nu,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\eta

(A.15)

and the Minkowski inequality.

Exercise A.3

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
A.1
prove that for all
\mu\in(0,1/2]
there exist random
F^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
such that

\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1/2]}\,[\mu]^{-\varrho_{3\varepsilon}(i,m)}\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast(F^{i,m}_{0,\mu}-F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}=0

(A.16)

almost surely. Hint: Use the argument from the proof of Lemma
A.1
.

Remark A.3

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Using the result stated in the above exercise and the fact that every
\kappa\in(0,1]
the coefficients
((0,1/2]\ni\mu\mapsto F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\in\mathcal{V}^{m})_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfy the flow equation (
3.11
) one verifies that the coefficients
((0,1/2]\ni\mu\mapsto F^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m}))_{i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
are almost surely continuously differentiable and satisfy the flow equation.

Appendix B
Relation to original equation and convergence

The lemma below specifies the conditions under which a fixed point of the map
\mathbf{Q}
, introduced in Lemma
2.15
, corresponds to a solution of
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
for
\kappa\in(0,1]
.

Remark B.1

(
\spadesuit
)
.

In the case of parabolic equations a natural way to proceed would be to first construct the maximal classical solution of the mild formulation of the SPDE with regularized noise
\xi_{\kappa}
by patching together solutions constructed in short time intervals using the contraction principle. Then it is straightforward to verify that the maximal solution restricted to a short time interval is a fixed point of an analogue of the map
\mathbf{Q}
introduced in Lemma
2.15
. In the case of elliptic equations the classical solution of
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
constructed using the contraction principle exists only for
\lambda\in[-\lambda_{\star,\kappa},\lambda_{\star,\kappa}]
, where
\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\lambda_{\star,\kappa}=0
. Because of this, we proceed in opposite direction and argue that for some
\lambda_{\star}\in(0,\infty)
independent of
\kappa\in(0,1]
and all
\lambda\in[-\lambda_{\star},\lambda_{\star}]
and
\kappa\in(0,1]
a fixed point of the map
\mathbf{Q}
, under some extra assumptions, is also a solution of
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
. It seems impossible to construct the above
\varPhi_{\kappa}
for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
by directly solving
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
.

Lemma B.2

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Fix
\kappa\in(0,1]
. Assume that the family of functionals
(F_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in[0,1]}
of polynomial type depends continuously on
\mu\in[0,1]
and is piecewise continuously differentiable in
\mu
for
\mu\in(0,1]
. Moreover, suppose that
F_{\kappa,0}=F_{\kappa}
and for some
R\in[1,\infty)
and
m_{\flat}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

\|\mathrm{D}^{k}(\partial_{\mu}^{s}F_{\kappa,\mu})[\varphi]\cdot\psi^{\otimes k}\,\|\leq R\,\|\psi\|^{k}\,(1+\|\varphi\|)^{m_{\flat}}

(B.1)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
s\in\{0,1\}
,
\mu\in[0,1]
,
\varphi,\psi\in C(\mathbb{T})
. Let the family of functionals
(H_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}
be defined by (
2.8
). If a continuous and bounded function

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,\mu})\in C(\mathbb{T})\times C(\mathbb{T})

(B.2)

is the fixed point of the map
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
defined by (
2.27
) in terms of

\tilde{F}_{\mu}[\varphi]:=K_{\mu}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}[K_{\mu}\ast\varphi]
and
\tilde{H}_{\mu}[\varphi]:=K_{\mu}\ast H_{\kappa,\mu}[K_{\mu}\ast\varphi]
, then the limit
\lim_{\mu\searrow 0}K_{\mu}\ast\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu}=:\varPhi_{\kappa}
exists in
C(\mathbb{T})
and satisfies the equation
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
.

Sketch of the proof.

Noting that
\mathbf{P}_{\mu}K_{\mu}=\delta_{0}
and
K_{\eta,\mu}\ast K_{\mu}=K_{\eta}
we show that

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto(\varPhi_{\kappa,\mu},\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}):=(K_{\mu}\ast\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu},\mathbf{P}_{\mu}\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,\mu})\in C(\mathbb{T})\times\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M})

(B.3)

satisfies the system of equations

\begin{cases}\varPhi_{\kappa,\mu}=-\int_{\mu}^{1}\dot{G}_{\eta}\ast(F_{\kappa,\eta}[\varPhi_{\kappa,\eta}]+\zeta_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta\\
\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}=-\int_{0}^{\mu}(H_{\kappa,\eta}[\varPhi_{\kappa,\eta}]+\mathrm{D}F_{\kappa,\eta}[\varPhi_{\kappa,\eta}]\cdot(\dot{G}_{\eta}\ast\zeta_{\kappa,\eta}))\,\mathrm{d}\eta.\end{cases}

(B.4)

Using the assumptions about the effective force and the fixed point we show that the integrands above are continuous and bounded. Hence, we conclude that

(0,1]\ni\mu\mapsto(\varPhi_{\kappa,\mu},\zeta_{\kappa,\mu})\in C(\mathbb{T})\times C(\mathbb{T})

(B.5)

and the above function is bounded, differentiable and has a limit at
\mu=0
. Next, we show that
\partial_{\eta}(F_{\kappa,\eta}[\varPhi_{\kappa,\eta}]+\zeta_{\kappa,\eta})=0
by following the argument from the beginning of Section
2
. As a result, we obtain
F_{\kappa,\eta}[\varPhi_{\kappa,\eta}]+\zeta_{\kappa,\eta}=F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
. Consequently, the first of the equations (
B.4
) implies that
\varPhi_{\kappa}
satisfies the equation
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
.
∎

In order to use the above lemma one has to verify that for
\kappa\in(0,1]
the unique fixed point
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})
of the map
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}\,:\,\mathscr{B}_{R}\to\mathscr{B}_{R}
constructed in Lemma
2.15
is such that the map (
B.2
) is bounded. The proof of this fact is the subject of the following exercise.

Exercise B.1

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Fix
\kappa\in(0,1]
. Assume that
\|\xi_{\kappa}\|\lesssim 1
and
|c^{(i)}_{\kappa}|\lesssim 1
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}
. Using the flow equation (
3.11
) show by induction that

\|F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|\lesssim 1\wedge[\mu]^{\sigma(m-3)/2}

uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
, where the constants of proportionality depend only on
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
. Conclude that there exists
R\in[1,\infty)
such that for every
\delta\in[-\sigma/2,0]
it holds

[\mu]^{-3\delta}\,\|F_{\kappa,\mu}[\varphi]\|\leq R\,(1+[\mu]^{-\delta}\,\|\varphi\|)^{3i_{\flat}},

(B.6)

where
F_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}
is the effective force defined by (
3.40
). Let
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})\in\mathscr{B}_{R}
be the unique fixed point of
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
constructed in Lemma
2.15
applied with
\alpha\in(-\sigma/2,\sigma-d/2)
. Using iteratively the bound (
B.6
) and the fact that
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})
is a fixed point of
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
show that
\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}\,:\,(0,1]\to C(\mathbb{T})
is bounded.

To conclude the proof of Theorem
1.1
one has to solve the following exercise in which we study the existence of the limit
\kappa\searrow 0
.

Exercise B.2

(
\spadesuit
)
.

For
\kappa\in[0,1]
let

(\tilde{F}_{\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}\equiv(\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]},\qquad(\tilde{H}_{\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}\equiv(\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}

(B.7)

be families of functionals such that:

(1)

for all
\kappa\in(0,1]
the assumptions of Lemma
B.2
are satisfied,

(2)

for all
\kappa\in[0,1]
the assumptions of Lemma
2.15
are satisfied,

(3)

for all
\kappa\in[0,1]
there exists
r_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle{}^{\sigma-\alpha}\,\|\mathrm{D}^{k}(\tilde{F}_{0,\mu}-\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu})[\varphi]\cdot\psi^{\otimes k}\|

\displaystyle\leq r_{\kappa}\,(\lambda^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\psi\|)^{k}\,(1/2+\lambda^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\varphi\|)^{m_{\flat}},

(B.8)

\displaystyle[\mu]^{\sigma-\beta}\,\|(\tilde{H}_{0,\mu}-\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu})[\varphi]\|

\displaystyle\leq r_{\kappa}\,(1/2+\lambda^{1/3}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|\varphi\|)^{m_{\flat}}

for all
k\in\{0,1\}
,
\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]
,
\varphi,\psi\in C(\mathbb{T})
,
\lambda\in[-1,1]
and
\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}r_{\kappa}=0
.

For
\kappa\in[0,1]
let
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
be defined by (
2.27
) in terms of the functionals (
B.7
). By Assumption (2) and Lemma
2.15
for every
\lambda\in[-\lambda_{\star},\lambda_{\star}]
and
\kappa\in[0,1]
the map
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}\,:\,\mathscr{B}_{R}\to\mathscr{B}_{R}
is well defined and is a contraction with the Lipschitz constant less than
1/2
. For
\kappa\in[0,1]
let
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})\in\mathscr{B}_{R}
be the unique fixed point of
\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}
. By Assumption (1), Lemma
B.2
and Exercise
B.1
the limit
\varPhi_{\kappa}:=\lim_{\mu\searrow 0}\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu}\in C_{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{M})
exists and solves
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
.

(i)

Show that

\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\mathbf{Q}_{\kappa}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}]=\mathbf{Q}_{0}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}}]
for all
(\tilde{\varPhi}_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})\in\mathscr{B}_{R}
.

(ii)

Using Item (i) conclude that
\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}(\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})=(\tilde{\varPhi}_{0,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}},\tilde{\zeta}_{0,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}})
.

(iii)

Verify the equality
K_{\mu}\ast\varPhi_{\kappa}=K_{\mu}\ast K_{\mu}\ast\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu}+(G-G_{\mu})\ast(\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu}[\tilde{\varPhi}_{\kappa,\mu}]+\tilde{\zeta}_{\kappa,\mu})
.

(iv)

Prove that there exists
\varPhi_{0}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M})
such that

\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\|\varPhi_{\kappa}-\varPhi_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{M})}=\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-\alpha}\,\|K_{\mu}\ast(\varPhi_{\kappa}-\varPhi_{0})\|=0.

(B.9)

Hints: To prove (iii) follow the proof of Lemma
B.2
. To prove (iv) use
\|G-G_{\mu}\|\lesssim[\mu]^{\sigma}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1

\mathrm{(}\spadesuit\mathrm{)}
.

First note that by Theorem
5.3
and Exercise
A.3
the assumption of Lemma
A.1
is satisfied.
By Lemma
A.1
and Exercise
A.3
one shows along the lines of the proof of Corollary
3.17
that the families of functionals
(\tilde{F}_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}
and
(\tilde{H}_{\kappa,\mu})_{\mu\in(0,1]}
defined by (
3.40
), (
3.3
) and (
3.2
) fulfil all the assumptions formulated in Exercise
B.2
. This implies Theorem
1.1
.
∎

Appendix C
Alternative proof of stochastic estimates

In this appendix, we give another proof of the stochastic estimates stated in Theorem
3.14
, which is not based on the bounds for the joint cumulants of the effective force coefficients. The proof is an alternative to the proof contained in Sections
4
–
5
and Appendix
A
and can be read independently. The idea of the proof is to estimate separately the expected values of the coefficients and their covariances. To bound the covariances we use the following simple lemma, which is a consequence of the the support property of the decomposition of the Green function
G
stated in Remark
2.10
.

Remark C.1

(
\spadesuit
)
.

In order to generalize the proof of the stochastic estimates presented in this section to scale decompositions of the Green function
G
that do not posses the support property stated in Remark
2.10
one would have to estimate the Malliavin derivative of the effective force coefficients in an appropriate weighted space.

Lemma C.2
.

For all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that for all
s\in\{0,1\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in[0,1/2]
and
x\in\mathbb{M}
,
A\in\mathrm{Borel}(\mathbb{M}^{m})
the random variable

\partial_{\mu}^{s}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;A+x)

is measurable with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra generated by

\{\langle\xi,\psi\rangle\,|\,\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}),~\textstyle\forall_{y\in\mathbb{M},|y-x|>c\,[\kappa\vee\mu]}\,\psi(y)=0\}.

(C.1)

Proof.

Since
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
if
m>3i
, the lemma is clearly true for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
m>3i
. To prove the lemma for
i=0
it is enough to study
F^{0,0}_{\kappa,\mu}=\xi_{\kappa}=\vartheta_{\kappa}\ast\xi
. By assumption
\mathrm{supp}\,\vartheta_{\kappa}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|\leq[\kappa]\}
. This implies the statement. The rest of the proof is by induction and is based on the flow equation (
3.11
), the fact that
\dot{G}_{\mu}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|\leq[\mu]\}
and the support property of the effective force coefficients stated in Lemma
3.18
.
∎

We replace Definitions
3.10
and
2.7
by the following definitions, which are more convenient in the present setting.

Definition C.3
.

Let
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
and
\alpha\equiv\alpha_{\varepsilon}:=\sigma-d/2-3\varepsilon
,
\gamma\equiv\gamma_{\varepsilon}:=3\sigma-d-9\varepsilon
. For
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define

\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m):=\alpha_{\varepsilon}-\sigma-m\,\alpha_{\varepsilon}+i\,\gamma_{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}.

(C.2)

We omit
\varepsilon
if
\varepsilon=0
. We define
i_{\flat},i_{\sharp},i_{\natural}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
to be the smallest positive integers such that
\varrho(i_{\flat}+1,0)>0
,
\varrho(i_{\sharp}+1,1)>0
,
\varrho(i_{\natural}+1,2)>0
, respectively.

Definition C.4
.

Let
\mu\in[0,1]
and
[\mu]=\mu^{1/\sigma}
. The kernel
\tilde{K}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{K}
is the unique solution of
\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}\tilde{K}_{\mu}=\delta_{0}
, where
\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}:=(1-[\mu]^{2}\Delta)^{d+2}
. We define
K_{\mu}:=\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{K}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{K}
and
\mathbf{P}_{\mu}:=\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}^{2}
. Let
v\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
v(r)=1
for
|r|\leq 1
and
v(r)=0
for
|r|\geq 2
. Define
\check{K}_{\mu},\hat{K}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{K}
by
\check{K}_{\mu}(x):=v(|x|/[\mu])\,\tilde{K}_{\mu}(x)
,
\hat{K}_{\mu}(x):=v(|x|/[\mu])\,K_{\mu}(x)
.

Remark C.5
.

Note that
\alpha_{\varepsilon}\leq 0
for all
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
. Moreover,
\gamma_{\varepsilon}>0
for all
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\varepsilon=0
by the condition of subcriticality. In particular,
i_{\flat},i_{\sharp}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
are well defined. For arbitrary
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
m\leq 3i
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)<\varrho(i,m)
.

To prove the bound for
\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}
we will decompose
\mathbb{E}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;\mathrm{d}y)
into a local part proportional to
\delta_{x}(\mathrm{d}y)
and a certain remainder using the maps introduced in the following definition.

Definition C.6
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we define
\delta^{[m]}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
by the equality

\langle\delta^{[m]},\psi\otimes\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle:=\int_{\mathbb{M}}\psi(x)\varphi_{1}(x)\ldots\varphi_{m}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

(C.3)

for all
\psi,\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{M})
. Let
\mathcal{X}^{a}(x;y):=(x-y)^{a}
for
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
and let
V\in\mathcal{V}^{1}
be such that
V(x;\mathrm{d}y)=V(0;\mathrm{d}(y-x))
,
V(x;\mathrm{d}y)=V(-x;\mathrm{d}(-y))
and
\mathcal{X}^{a}V\in\mathcal{V}^{1}
for all
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
. We define
\mathbf{I}V:=\int_{\mathbb{M}}V(x;\mathrm{d}y)\in\mathbb{R}
. For
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
we define
\mathbf{R}^{a}V\in\mathcal{V}^{1}
by the equality

(\mathbf{R}^{a}V)(x;\mathrm{d}y):=\frac{|a|}{a!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{|a|-1}/\tau^{d}~(\mathcal{X}^{a}V)(x;\mathrm{d}(x+(y-x)/\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(C.4)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{M}
.

Exercise C.1
.

Prove that the following equality
V=(\mathbf{I}V)\,\delta^{[1]}+\sum_{|a|=2}\partial^{a}\mathbf{R}^{a}V
holds in
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{2})
, where the sum is over
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
. Show that
|\mathbf{I}V|\leq\|V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}
and
\|\mathbf{R}^{a}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\leq\|\mathcal{X}^{a}V\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}
. Hint: Use the integral form of the Taylor remainder.

Lemma C.7
.

Let
d\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
and
\sigma\in(d/3,d/2]
. There exist a choice of the counterterms
(c^{(i)}_{\kappa})_{i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}}
in the expression (
1.7
) for the force
F_{\kappa}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
,
r,s\in\{0,1\}
it holds

\big(\mathbb{E}\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\big)^{1/n}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\kappa\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-(\sigma-\varepsilon)s-\sigma(3i-m-s)}\,[\mu]^{\sigma(3i-m-s)}

(C.5)

uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1/2]
.

Remark C.8
.

For simplicity we assume that
d\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
. The case
d\in\{5,6\}
requires one minor modification. For
d>6
other counterterms are needed to renormalize the nonlinearity.

Exercise C.2
.

Show that
\varrho(i,m)-\sigma(3i-m)\leq 0
for all
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
m\leq 2i+1
.

Remark C.9
.

Since
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
for
m>2i+1
by Exercise
3.2
the above lemma together with the result of the above exercise imply that for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
,
r,s\in\{0,1\}
it holds

\big(\mathbb{E}\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\big)^{1/n}\lesssim[\kappa]^{(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma s}

(C.6)

uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. Using Exercise
A.2
and Lemma
C.12
(B) we infer that there exists a random variable
\tilde{R}\in[1,\infty]
such that
\mathbb{E}\tilde{R}^{n}<\infty
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and it holds

\sup_{\kappa\in(0,1]}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1/2]}[\mu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\tilde{R}

(C.7)

for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
. Furthermore, for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
,
m\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
,
\mu\in(0,1/2]
there exists random
F^{i,m}_{0,\mu}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{M}^{1+m})
such that

\lim_{\kappa\searrow 0}\sup_{\mu\in(0,1/2]}[\mu]^{-\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast(F^{i,m}_{0,\mu}-F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}=0.

(C.8)

This in particular proves Theorem
3.14
.

Remark C.10
.

Note that the equation we want to solve,
\varPhi_{\kappa}=G\ast F_{\kappa}[\varPhi_{\kappa}]
, is invariant under the transformations
(\varPhi_{\kappa},\xi_{\kappa})\mapsto-(\varPhi_{\kappa},\xi_{\kappa})
and
(\varPhi_{\kappa},\xi_{\kappa})\mapsto(\varPhi_{\kappa}(-\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}),\xi_{\kappa}(-\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}}))
. Using the fact that the law of
\xi_{\kappa}
is invariant under the transformations
\xi_{\kappa}\mapsto-\xi_{\kappa}
and
\xi_{\kappa}\mapsto\xi_{\kappa}(-\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.6}{\hskip 1.42262pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 1.42262pt}}}}}})
one shows that
\mathbb{E}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=0
unless
m
is odd and
\mathbb{E}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
is invariant under the inversion through the origin
0\in\mathbb{M}^{1+m}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

For simplicity, we give the proof only for
r=0
. Note that by the Jensen inequality it is enough to prove the lemma for sufficiently big
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. The proof is by induction on
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

We start with the
base case

i=0
. Hence,
m=0
and
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=\xi_{\kappa}
. It holds

\mathbb{E}((\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\xi_{\kappa})(x)^{2})\lesssim[\mu]^{-d}

(C.9)

by Lemma
4.19
(D) applied with
p=\infty
. By the Nelson estimate we obtain

\mathbb{E}((\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\xi_{\kappa})(x)^{n})\lesssim[\mu]^{-nd/2}

(C.10)

for all
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Since
K_{\mu}=\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{K}_{\mu}
by Kolmogorov-type estimate stated in Lemma
C.13
we obtain

\mathbb{E}\|K_{\mu}\ast\xi_{\kappa}\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-nd/2-d},

(C.11)

which implies the statement of the lemma provided
n\varepsilon>d
. This finishes the proof of the base case.

We proceed to the proof of the
induction step
. Fix
i_{\circ}\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\flat}\}
and
m_{\circ}\in\{0,\ldots,3i\}
and assuming that the lemma is true for all
i<i_{\circ}
, and
i=i_{\circ}
and
m>m_{\circ}
. The goal is to prove the lemma for
i=i_{\circ}
and
m=m_{\circ}
. Let us observe that the flow equation (
3.11
) implies

K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\mu}F_{\kappa,\mu}^{i,m}=-\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\,(1+k)\,\mathbf{B}(\tilde{G}_{\mu},K_{\mu}^{\otimes(2+k)}\ast F^{j,1+k}_{\kappa,\mu},K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m-k)}\ast F^{i-j,m-k}_{\kappa,\mu}),

(C.12)

where
\tilde{G}_{\mu}=\mathbf{P}_{\mu}^{2}\dot{G}_{\mu}
.
Consequently, the statement with
s=1
follows from the bound stated in Exercise
3.1
, Lemma
2.11
as well as the identities

\begin{gathered}\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon=\varrho_{\varepsilon}(j,1+k)+\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i-j,m-k),\\
\sigma\,(3i-m-1)=\sigma\,(3j-(1+k))+\sigma\,(3(i-j)-(m-k)).\end{gathered}

(C.13)

It remains to establish the statement with
s=0
. In order to prove the statement of the lemma for
s=0
and
i,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\varrho(i,m)>0
we use the identity

F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(C.14)

We first observe that
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}=0
if
\varrho(i,m)>0
. Next, we note that

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}

(C.15)

for
\eta\leq\mu
by Exercise
2.1
(3). The statement of the lemma with
s=0
follows now from the statement with
s=1
and the bounds

\big(\mathbb{E}\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\big)^{1/n}\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\big(\mathbb{E}\|K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\big)^{1/n}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(C.16)

and

\int_{0}^{\mu}[\kappa\vee\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon-\sigma(3i-m-1)}\,[\eta]^{\sigma(3i-m-1)}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\kappa\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+\sigma(3i-m)}[\eta]^{\sigma(3i-m)}.

(C.17)

We stress that the above bound is valid uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in(0,1/2]
if
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)>0
, which holds provided
\varrho(i,m)>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
is sufficiently small. We also note that the bound is valid uniformly in
\kappa\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in(0,\kappa]
irrespective of the sign of
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)
. Consequently, it remains to prove the statement for
s=0
,
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
and
\kappa\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in(\kappa,1]
.

To proceed, let us recall the non-zero force coefficients

F^{0,0}_{\kappa}=\xi_{\kappa},\quad F^{1,3}_{\kappa}=\delta^{[3]},\quad F^{1,1}_{\kappa}=c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\,\delta^{[1]},\quad i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}.

(C.18)

In particular
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}=F^{i,m}_{\kappa}
is deterministic if
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Note that
\varrho(i,m)>0
if
m\geq 4
. For
m=3
the condition
\varrho(i,m)\leq 0
implies
i=1
. We have

\partial_{\eta}F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\eta}=0,\qquad F^{1,3}_{\kappa,0}=\delta^{[3]}.

(C.19)

Consequently,

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes 4}\ast F^{1,3}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{3}}=1,

(C.20)

which implies the statement. For
m\in\{0,1,2\}
we decompose

\displaystyle F^{i,0}_{\kappa,\mu}

\displaystyle=\tilde{F}^{i,0}_{\kappa,\mu}+\check{F}^{i,0}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\flat}\},

(C.21)

\displaystyle F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\,\delta^{[1]}+\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}+\tilde{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu}+\check{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\},

\displaystyle F^{i,2}_{\kappa,\mu}

\displaystyle=\tilde{F}^{i,2}_{\kappa,\mu}+\check{F}^{i,2}_{\kappa,\mu},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\natural}\},

where

\begin{split}\partial_{\eta}\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\mathbf{I}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta}),\\
\partial_{\eta}\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\textstyle\sum_{|a|=2}\partial^{a}\mathbf{R}^{a}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta}),\\
\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}-\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta},\\
\partial_{\eta}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}&:=\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}-\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta},\end{split}\qquad\qquad\begin{split}\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}&:=c_{\kappa}^{(i)},\\
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}&:=0,\\
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}&:=0,\\
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,0}&:=0\end{split}

(C.22)

and

\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}:=K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}.

(C.23)

The motivation behind the above decomposition is that we will be able to prove bounds for
\partial_{\eta}\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
,
\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}
and
\partial_{\eta}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}
that are integrable in
\eta
at
\eta=0
. On the other hand, the bound for
\partial_{\eta}\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\,\delta^{[1]}
is not integrable in
\eta
at
\eta=0
. The bound for
\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}\delta^{[1]}
relies crucially on the fact that this term is local and is only valid for suitable choices of the counterterm
c_{\kappa}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}
.

We shall first bound
\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}
and then proceed to
\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}
,
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}
and
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
. Note that

\partial_{\eta}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}=(\delta_{0}^{\otimes(1+m)}-K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)})\ast\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}.

(C.24)

Using the above identity and the properties of the kernels
K_{\eta}
one shows that

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\eta/\mu]^{2}\,\|K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}.

(C.25)

Note that for
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+2>0
for all sufficiently small
\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)
. Here we used the assumption that
d\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
. Consequently, we obtain

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\check{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{2}\,\|K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(C.26)

and by the bound stated in the lemma with
s=1
, which was proved above, we arrive at

\big(\mathbb{E}\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast F_{\kappa,\mu}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\big)^{1/n}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{2}\,\big(\mathbb{E}\|K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,m}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}^{n}\big)^{1/n}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\\
[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon+2}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}.

(C.27)

Next, let us study the local and deterministic term
\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\,\delta^{[1]}
. First, observe that by the bound stated in the lemma with
s=1
we have

\big(\mathbb{E}\|\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\big)^{1/n}\lesssim[\kappa\vee\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-(\sigma-\varepsilon)-\sigma(3i-m-1)}\,[\eta]^{\sigma(3i-m-1)}\\
\lesssim[\kappa\vee\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon}\lesssim[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon},

(C.28)

where the second to last estimate is true because
3i-m-1\geq 0
unless
\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}=0
and the last estimate is true because we study the case
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)\leq 0
. We fix the counterterm to be

c_{\kappa}^{(i)}:=-\int_{0}^{1/2}\mathbf{I}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(C.29)

Then the following identity

\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}=c_{\kappa}^{(i)}+\int_{0}^{\mu}\mathbf{I}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta=-\int_{\mu}^{1/2}\mathbf{I}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta})\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(C.30)

holds true. Consequently, since
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)<0
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,i_{\sharp}\}
using the bound (
C.28
) with
n=1
we obtain

|\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}|\lesssim\int_{\mu}^{1/2}\,[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)-\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)}.

(C.31)

This implies the desired bound

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast(\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\,\delta^{[1]})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)}.

(C.32)

Let us proceed to the estimates for the non-local deterministic term
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\eta}
. Using the boundary condition
\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,0}=0
and the bound
\|\partial^{a}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-|a|}
proved in Lemma
C.12
(A) we estimate

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\check{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\sup_{|a|=2}\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\mathbf{R}^{a}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(C.33)

By Exercise
C.1
the RHS is bounded up to a constant by

\sup_{|a|=2}~[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(C.34)

Recall that by Lemma
3.18
there exists
c\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\eta\in(0,1/2]
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,\partial_{\eta}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta}\subset\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{M}^{2}\,|\,|x-y|\leq c\,[\eta]\}.

(C.35)

Pretending that there exist
c\in(0,\infty)
such that for

\mathrm{supp}\,K_{\eta}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<c\,[\eta]\}

(C.36)

for all
\eta\in(0,1/2]
we obtain that
\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}=K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}
has the same support property as
\partial_{\eta}F^{i,1}_{\kappa,\eta}
. Consequently, for
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
such that
|a|=2
it holds

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,1})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\lesssim[\eta]^{2}\,\|\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}.

(C.37)

Since the kernel
K_{\eta}
does not have the above-mentioned support property the rigorous proof of the above estimate is slightly more complicated and is given in Lemma
C.14
below. Taking into account the bound (
C.28
) we obtain the bound

\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes 2}\ast\hat{E}^{i}_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{2}\,\|\mathbb{E}\mathring{F}_{\kappa,\eta}^{i,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2}\,\mathrm{d}\eta.

(C.38)

To conclude we use the fact that
\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2>0
to show

[\mu]^{-2}\int_{0}^{\mu}[\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)+2}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\lesssim[\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,1)}.

(C.39)

Finally, we discuss the proof of the bound for
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}
.
By the Minkowski inequality we have

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\big)^{2}}\leq\int_{0}^{\mu}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\big)^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta\\
=\int_{0}^{\mu}\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\big(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\big)}\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(C.40)

Let us study the covariance

\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta},\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\big)
, where
\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}=K_{\eta}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}
. By Lemma
C.2
there exists
c\in(0,\infty)
such that

\mathrm{supp}\,\mathrm{Cov}\big(\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta},\partial_{\eta}F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\big)\subset\{(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m},x^{\prime},y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{m})\in\mathbb{M}^{2+2m}\,|\,|x-x^{\prime}|\leq c\,[\kappa\vee\eta]\}.

(C.41)

Moreover, by the bound (
C.28
) we have

\|\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta},\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}\big)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}^{2m+2m})}\lesssim[\kappa\vee\eta]^{2(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon)}.

(C.42)

As a result, pretending that the kernel
K_{\eta}
has the support property (
C.36
) we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{2m}}\big|\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}),\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}(x^{\prime};y^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{m})\big)\big|\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}y_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{m}\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}_{1}\ldots\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}_{m}\\
\lesssim[\kappa\vee\eta]^{2(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon)+d}.

(C.43)

To prove the above bound rigorously we use the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
C.14
. Consequently, by Lemma
C.12
(C), (D) applied with
p=\infty
it holds

\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\big(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\mathring{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\eta}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\big)}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d/2-dm}\,[\kappa\vee\eta]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-\sigma+\varepsilon+d/2}

(C.44)

and by the bound (
C.40
) we obtain

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\big)^{2}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d/2-dm}\,[\kappa\vee\mu]^{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+\varepsilon+d/2}.

(C.45)

From now on we assume that
\mu\in[\kappa,1]
. Recall that for
\mu\in(0,\kappa]
the statement has already been proved. Since
F^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
is in a finite Wiener chaos we can use the Nelson estimate to conclude that

\mathbb{E}\big(\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}(x;y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\big)^{n}\lesssim[\kappa\vee\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+\varepsilon-dm)}.

(C.46)

Using the Fubini theorem and pretending again that the kernels
\tilde{K}_{\mu}
and
K_{\mu}
have the support property (
C.36
) one shows that

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}\lesssim[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)+\varepsilon-dm)+dm}

(C.47)

To make the above argument precise one can, for example, use weights to show that
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
is essentially localized in a neighbourhood of the diagonal of diameter of order
[\mu]
. Since
K_{\mu}=\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\tilde{K}_{\mu}
by Kolmogorov-type estimate stated in Lemma
C.13
we obtain

\mathbb{E}\big\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\big\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}\lesssim[\mu]^{n(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)-dm)}

(C.48)

provided
n\varepsilon>d
. Employing again the fact that
\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}
is essentially localized in a neighbourhood of the diagonal of diameter of order
[\mu]
we obtain

\mathbb{E}\big\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\tilde{F}^{i,m}_{\kappa,\mu}\big\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\lesssim[\mu]^{n\varrho_{\varepsilon}(i,m)}.

(C.49)

Thus, we have estimated all the terms appearing in the decompositions (
C.21
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition C.11

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
\mathbb{T}:=\mathbb{M}/(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{d}
. For
K\in L^{1}(\mathbb{M})
we define
\mathbf{T}K\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T})
by

\mathbf{T}K(x):=\sum_{y\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{d}}K(x+y).

(C.50)

Lemma C.12

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
and
p\in[1,\infty]
. The following is true:

(A)

If
|a|\leq d
, then
\|\partial^{a}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-|a|}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(B)

It holds
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim[\mu]^{-\sigma}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(C)

\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{M})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d(p-1)/p}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

(D)

\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim[\mu]^{-d(p-1)/p}
uniformly in
\mu\in(0,1]
.

Lemma C.13

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Let
n\in 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all random fields
\zeta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
and
\mu\in(0,1]
it holds

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}\leq C\,[\mu]^{-d(1+m)}\,\mathbb{E}\|\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})}

(C.51)

Proof.

Note that

\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast\zeta=(\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\otimes\tilde{K}_{\mu}^{\otimes m})\star\zeta

(C.52)

where
\star
is the convolution in
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m}
and
\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}
is the periodization of
\tilde{K}_{\mu}
(see Def.
C.11
). Using the Young inequality for convolutions we obtain

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}\ast\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|\mathbf{T}\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}(\mathbb{T})}^{n}\,\|\tilde{K}_{\mu}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}(\mathbb{T})}^{mn}\,\mathbb{E}\|\zeta\|^{n}_{L^{n}(\mathbb{T})}.

(C.53)

The lemma follows now from Lemma
C.12
(B).
∎

Exercise C.3

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Prove that there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that if

\mathbb{E}\|\partial_{\mu}^{s}\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}^{\phantom{l}}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\\
\leq C\,[\kappa]^{n(-\sigma r+\varepsilon r)}[\mu]^{n(\rho-\sigma s+\varepsilon s)},\qquad s,r\in\{0,1\},~\kappa,\mu\in(0,1],

(C.54)

for some differentiable random function
\zeta:(0,1]^{2}\to\mathcal{V}^{m}
and
C>0
,
\rho\leq 0
, then

\mathbb{E}\Big(\sup_{\kappa,\mu\in(0,1]}[\mu]^{-n\rho}\,\|\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}\|^{n}_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\Big)\leq c\,C.

(C.55)

Hint: Use the estimate

[\mu]^{-\rho}\,\|\zeta_{\kappa,\mu}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\leq\|\zeta_{1,1}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}+\int_{\mu}^{1}[\eta]^{-\rho}\,\|\partial_{\eta}\zeta_{1,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\eta+\int_{\mu}^{1}\int_{\kappa}^{1}[\eta]^{-\rho}\,\|\partial_{\eta}\partial_{\nu}\zeta_{\nu,\eta}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\mathrm{d}\eta

(C.56)

and the Minkowski inequality.

Lemma C.14

(
\spadesuit
)
.

Fix some
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
. There exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that if for some
\mu\in(0,1/2]
and
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{M}^{m})
it holds

\mathrm{supp}\,V\subset\{(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\,|\,|x-y_{1}|\vee\ldots\vee|x-y_{m}|\leq c\,[\mu]\}

(C.57)

then

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\leq C\,[\mu]^{|a|}\,\|K_{\mu}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}.

(C.58)

Remark C.15
.

Note that the lemma would be obvious if
K_{\mu}\in C(\mathbb{M})
was compactly supported in a ball
\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<c\,[\mu]\}
for some
c\in(0,\infty)
independent of
\mu\in(0,1/2]
. Even though this is not exactly true, one can easily proved the above result by leveraging the fact that the kernel
K_{\mu}
decays exponentially with the rate
1/[\mu]
.

Proof
\mathrm{(}\spadesuit\mathrm{)}
.

A simple rescaling reduces the proof to the case
\mu=1
. Let
v\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{M})
be such that
\mathrm{supp}\,v\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<1\}
and
v=1
on
\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|\leq 1/2\}
. For
\tau\in[1,\infty)
let
L_{\tau}(x):=K_{1}(x)\,v(x/\tau)
. It holds

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(K_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\leq\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(L_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\|\mathcal{X}^{a}\partial_{\tau}(L_{\tau}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\|L_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\tau^{|a|}\,\|\partial_{\tau}(L_{\tau}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(C.59)

where to get the last estimate we used the fact that
\mathrm{supp}\,L_{\tau}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{M}\,|\,|x|<\tau\}
. Next, we observe that

L_{\tau}=\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}L_{\tau}\ast K_{1},\qquad\partial_{\tau}L_{\tau}=\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\partial_{\tau}L_{\tau}\ast K_{1}

(C.60)

and
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}L_{\tau}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim 1
and
\|\tilde{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\partial_{\tau}L_{\tau}\|_{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim\tau^{-N}
uniformly in
\tau\in[1,\infty)
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
because of the exponential decay of the kernel
K_{1}
. Consequently, we have

\|\mathcal{X}^{a}(K_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\lesssim\|K_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\tau^{|a|-N}\,\|K_{1}^{\otimes(1+m)}\ast V\|_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{t}}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(C.61)

which finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.16

(Nelson’s estimate)
.

For every random variable
X
in an inhomogeneous Wiener chaos of order
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and every
p\in[2,\infty)
it holds

\big(\mathbb{E}|X|^{p}\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq(p-1)^{\frac{n}{2}}\,\big(\mathbb{E}X^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(C.62)

Proof.

The bound follows from the Nelson hypercontractivity of the Ornstein–Uhlenbeck operator (see e.g.
[
Nua06
, Theorem 1.4.1]
).
∎

Exercise C.4
.

Complete the proof of Lemma
C.7
by checking that all of the steps generalize to the case
r=1
. To prove the base case of the induction verify that

\mathbb{E}((K_{\mu}\ast\partial_{\kappa}^{r}\xi_{\kappa})(x)^{2})\lesssim[\kappa]^{2(\varepsilon-\sigma)r}\,[\mu]^{-2(d/2+\varepsilon)}.

(C.63)
